# Decisions

Shared, agent-authored log of cross-cutting decisions the fleet must honor. The conductor records plan/steer decisions here; workers record interface contracts + adjustments. Re-read before each significant step.

- **Why:** user-approved plan; workers implement these nodes in isolated workspaces, honoring the dependency edges

## n0-2: Web light theme foundation — token contract for downstream workers
- **What:** globals.css/tailwind.config.ts/layout.tsx are now light-themed. ALL class and token names are unchanged; only values changed. Contract for page/component workers:
  - `--bg` #ffffff, `--bg-2` #f7f8fa (panel gray), `--foreground` #1e2530, `--muted` #6b7280, `--muted-2` #9ca3af.
  - `--surface` and `--border` are still RGB **channels** but flipped from white to dark slate (`30 37 48` / `31 41 55`): `rgb(var(--surface)/0.05)` now yields a subtle gray tint on white, `rgb(var(--border)/0.1)` ≈ #e5e7eb hairline. Any component using raw `bg-white/5`, `border-white/10` etc. must be migrated — white-alpha overlays are invisible on the light canvas.
  - Brand ramp is the blue accent: `--brand-1` #3b82f6, `--brand-2` #2563eb (DEFAULT), `--brand-3` #1d4ed8. Neon names are semantic now: mint=#16a34a (yes/live), rose=#dc2626 (no), amber=#d97706, cyan=#0284c7, violet=#4f46e5.
  - `.glass`/`.glass-strong` = white bordered cards with subtle shadows; `.glass-subtle` = --bg-2 panel; `.btn-primary` = solid blue pill; `.btn-glass` = white bordered pill; chips = light tinted pills; `.text-gradient`/`.text-gradient-cool` = solid ink/blue (no longer transparent-clip). shadow-glow* tailwind tokens are now subtle light-theme shadows.
  - `.aurora` is `display:none` and the div is removed from layout.tsx; html no longer has the `dark` class (tailwind `dark:` variants no longer apply). themeColor is #ffffff.
- **Why:** keeps every downstream file compiling and rendering sensibly during the light-mode migration
- **By:** n0-2 · 2026-07-13

## n0-2: Re-themed the web design system foundation from dark Midnight
- **Did:** Re-themed the web design system foundation from dark Midnight Glass to a light Polymarket-like system. app/globals.css: :root vars redefined for light mode (white bg, #f7f8fa panels, dark slate text, blue brand ramp #3b82f6/#2563eb/#1d4ed8, neon-* mapped to semantic light accents green/red/amber/blues), --surface/--border RGB channels flipped from white to dark slate so existing rgb(var/alpha) overlays become gray tints on white, color-scheme:light; .glass* utilities are now white bordered cards with subtle shadows, .btn-primary a solid blue pill, .btn-glass white bordered, chips light tinted pills, .text-gradient(-cool) solid ink/blue, skeleton+scrollbar light, .aurora neutralized to display:none. tailwind.config.ts: shadow-glow* tokens softened to subtle light shadows, glass-sheen near-invisible; all token names kept. app/layout.tsx: removed dark class from html, removed aurora div, themeColor #ffffff. Verified: npx tsc --noEmit clean, npm run build succeeds (needed .env.local copied from main checkout for Stack Auth page-data collection; gitignored, not in diff). jj diff touches only the 3 owned files.
- **Interfaces:** Token contract recorded in DECISIONS.md: all class/var names unchanged, values light. Key gotcha for siblings: --surface/--border are now dark-slate RGB channels, so raw bg-white/5 or border-white/10 utilities in components are invisible on the light canvas and must be migrated; dark: variants no longer apply since html lost the dark class.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - Migrate raw white-alpha utilities in components [out of lane] — ~52 uses of bg-white/N and border-white/N in app/ and components/ render invisible on the light canvas; owned by page/component workers
  - Consider dropping Space Grotesk display font [out of lane] — font-display is kept loading in layout.tsx for compatibility; if downstream workers standardize on Inter for headings the extra font load can go

## n6: mobile/src/theme.ts is now the LIGHT Polymarket-like token set (same export shape)
- **What:** All exported names/shapes unchanged (colors.*, gradients.*, radius, spacing, glass.*, glow(), inputStyle) but values are now light: bg #FFFFFF, bg2 #F7F8FA, surface/surfaceElevated #FFFFFF, surfaceGlass rgba(15,23,42,0.04), border #E5E7EB, text #1E2530, textMuted #6B7280, textFaint #9CA3AF, brand/brand2 #2563EB (brand1 #3B82F6, brand3 #1D4ED8), mint #16A34A (YES), rose #DC2626 (NO), amber #D97706 (pending), cyan #0EA5E9, violet #4F46E5; *Fill tokens are 8% tints. glass.card/cardStrong are white cards with #E5E7EB border + soft slate shadow; glow() now ignores its color arg and returns a subtle neutral shadow; gradients.card is near-flat white. NavigationProgress.tsx: dropped shadow-glow-ember, kept the h-0.5 blue brand-gradient bar. MobileAppBanner.tsx: blue-gradient banner became a light bg-background-2 strip with border-b gray-200, blue icon, solid-blue CTA (bg-brand-2 hover:bg-brand-3 text-white), dismiss button muted with hover:bg-black/5. Verified: npx tsc --noEmit exit 0 (root node_modules APFS-cloned from main checkout into worktree, gitignored, not in diff); grep sweep of the four files shows no border-white/*, bg-white/[0.0x], bg-background/*, neon-* classes, drop-shadows, or shadow-glow* — only the sanctioned bg-white/90 / bg-white/95 chrome surfaces; jj status shows only the 4 owned files modified.

Consumed n0-2 tokens as-is (text-brand-2, bg-brand-2/3, bg-background-2, glass-strong, btn-primary/btn-glass, bg-brand-gradient). Note for siblings: avoided Tailwind alpha modifiers on var()-backed token colors (e.g. bg-brand-2/15, bg-neon-mint/10) since the config defines them without <alpha-value> — used standard palette equivalents (green-600, blue-50, red-600) which match the contract hexes exactly.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - Tailwind var() colors do not support alpha modifiers [out of lane] — tailwind.config.ts defines brand/neon colors as plain var(--x) hex strings, so classes like bg-brand-2/15 or bg-neon-mint/10 silently drop the alpha; other components still using that pattern render solid instead of tinted. Foundation could redefine tokens as RGB channels with <alpha-value>.
  - MobileAppBanner is dormant until a real TestFlight link is set [out of lane] — HAS_TESTFLIGHT_LINK is false (placeholder URL), so the restyled banner never renders in prod; visual check requires temporarily flipping the flag.

## n9: Terminal polish pass over the whole surface. Fixed the
- **Did:** Terminal polish pass over the whole surface. Fixed the one real rendering bug left after the re-theme: Tailwind brand/neon tokens were plain var() hexes so every alpha-modified class (37 sites, e.g. bg-brand-2/10, ring-brand-2/20, border-brand-2/50 across create/users/groups/events pages and BetForm/CommentForm/UsernameModal) silently dropped its alpha and painted SOLID #2563eb — admin chips rendered blue-on-blue, focus rings harsh solid blue. Fixed systemically: added --brand-*-rgb / --neon-*-rgb channel vars to globals.css and redefined the tailwind brand/neon colors as rgb(var(--x-rgb) / <alpha-value>); verified all tint variants (/.1-/.6, /0.2 ring) in the compiled CSS. Metadata: public/manifest.json theme_color #ea580c -> #ffffff; mobile/app.json splash backgroundColor #ea580c -> #ffffff and expo-notifications accent #ea580c -> #2563eb. Mobile follow-ups from n7/n8 closed: violetFill/amberFill tokens added to mobile/src/theme.ts and consumed in ActivityScreen/ProfileScreen (replacing hardcoded rgba), HomeScreen actionSubtitleAlt opacity 1. Full sweep found NO other residue: no white-alpha overlays beyond sanctioned bg-white/90-95 chrome, no dark hexes/dark utility classes/arbitrary hex classes/orange remnants in app+components+mobile/src+lib, color-scheme:light everywhere, yes=green/no=red/live=green consistent, modal scrims bg-black/40, all text-white on colored fills, muted text floors at #9ca3af. Verified: root npx tsc --noEmit 0, mobile npx tsc --noEmit 0, npm run build succeeds (needed .env.local + node_modules APFS-cloned from main checkout, both gitignored), prod server spot-check shows theme-color #ffffff / status-bar default / zero aurora in rendered home HTML.
- **Interfaces:** No new exports/props/types. globals.css: added --brand-{1,2,3}-rgb and --neon-{violet,cyan,mint,rose,amber}-rgb channel vars (hex vars kept, so direct var() consumers like btn-primary/live-dot/brand-gradient unchanged). tailwind.config.ts: brand.* and neon.* now rgb(var(--x-rgb) / <alpha-value>) — alpha modifiers on these tokens now WORK; unmodified classes (text-brand-2 etc.) compile to identical colors. mobile/src/theme.ts: added colors.violetFill and colors.amberFill.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - App icons still orange-branded [out of lane] — public/icons/*.svg and mobile assets (icon.png, splash-icon.png, adaptive-icon) keep the legacy orange #ea580c brand tile while the app accent is now blue #2563eb. Redrawing brand artwork was out of scope for a polish pass; needs a design decision.
  - Hoist repeated input styling into a shared .input class [out of lane] — calendar/profile/signin/create repeat bg-white border-gray-200 + blue focus ring inline (n5 also flagged); cosmetic tech-debt only.

## n3: Money contract — `payment_type` is the ONLY real-money switch
- **What:** Real money now flows exclusively through `events.payment_type === 'cash'`. The old rule ("a private group means real money", i.e. `!group.is_public`) is GONE from `app/api/bets/route.ts` and `app/api/events/resolve/route.ts`. A `payment_type: 'none'` event never touches a wallet in any group, public or private. Safe to land: every pre-existing event row is `payment_type = 'none'`, the transactions table was empty and every wallet balance was $0.00, so no existing row changed meaning.
- **New shared module `lib/payments.ts`** (I created it; `lib/db.ts` is n1-owned and already merged, and four routes needed one money engine rather than four copies). Every money mutation goes through it, each wrapped in a single `withTransaction`: `placeCashBet`, `settleCashEvent(eventId, winningSide | null)`, `reverseCashSettlement`, `creditStripeDeposit`, `failStripeDeposit`, `withdrawFromWallet`, `getWalletSummary`, plus `PaymentError` / `isPaymentError` / `MAX_TRANSACTION_AMOUNT`.
- **Ledger sign convention (do not deviate):** signs are from the wallet's point of view — `escrow_hold` = −stake, `escrow_release` = +stake, `payout` = +net winnings, `refund` = +stake, `deposit` = +amount, `withdrawal` = −amount. Every money row is written `status = 'completed'` with `event_id` set when it belongs to an event.
- **Two invariants any future change must preserve:** (1) `SUM(transactions.amount WHERE user_id = U AND status = 'completed')` === `wallets.balance` for U; (2) a settled cash event's `SUM(transactions.amount WHERE event_id = E AND status = 'completed')` === `0` with no `escrow_holds` left in `'held'`. `scripts/verify-payments.ts` (npm run verify:payments) proves both against the dev DB — 228 checks, all passing.
- **Idempotency keys** (unique partial index on `transactions.idempotency_key`, so inserts must use `ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING`): `bet:<holdId>`, `release:<eventId>:<userId>:<seq>`, `payout:<eventId>:<userId>:<seq>`, `refund:<eventId>:<holdId>:<seq>`, `reverse:<originalTxId>`, `stripe:<stripeEventId>`, `withdraw:<userId>:<clientKey>`. `<seq>` is a settlement generation counter — without it a resolve → unresolve → re-resolve cycle silently pays nobody and strands the pot.
- **API contract for siblings:** `POST /api/events` accepts `payment_type`, `stake_amount` (null = each bettor picks their own), and passes `subject_user_id` / `notify_subject` straight through (n4's fields — I made the route not drop them so n4 does not have to touch my file). `GET /api/events?id=` gained `escrow_total`. `GET /api/wallet?userId=&eventId=` gained `escrow_held_total`, `available` and an `event` block; `wallet` and `transactions` stay at the top level. `POST /api/events/resolve` accepts `{ event_id, action: 'cancel' }` to refund every stake. Money errors return `{ error, code, ...details }` — clients should branch on `code` (`INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` carries `balance`/`required`/`shortfall`), never on the message.
- **A cancelled event is `status = 'resolved'` with `winning_side IS NULL`** (no magic string, no new enum value, so `lib/types.ts` did not need changing). The web client keys its "cancelled" banner off exactly that.
- **Deliberate behaviour changes:** cash events reject late bets (escrowed money would be stranded, since `calculateNetResults` ignores late bets); bets on cash events cannot be deleted (`CASH_BET_IMMUTABLE`) — cancel the event to refund instead; `app/api/events/delete` was unauthenticated and now requires auth + the resolver/admin rule, and refuses to delete a cash event with outstanding escrow (the `escrow_holds` CASCADE would have destroyed real money).
- **By:** n3 · 2026-08-16
## n3: Real-money bets now actually move money, end to end,
- **Did:** Real-money bets now actually move money, end to end, proven by a runnable script. New lib/payments.ts is the single money engine: placeCashBet, settleCashEvent, reverseCashSettlement, creditStripeDeposit, failStripeDeposit, withdrawFromWallet, getWalletSummary — each one call, one DB transaction, guarded debits so a wallet can never go negative (the CHECK constraint is never the thing that catches it). The real-money switch is now events.payment_type==='cash' ONLY; the old 'private group means real money' rule is gone (safe: every existing event is payment_type 'none', transactions was empty, all balances were $0). Creation: /create picks Just-for-fun vs Real money, fixed stake or open stakes; POST /api/events validates and persists payment_type/stake_amount (and passes n4's subject_user_id/notify_subject through so n4 need not touch my file). Betting: a cash bet debits the wallet, writes an escrow_holds row 'held', an escrow_hold transaction and the bet linked to the hold, all in one transaction; insufficient funds returns 400 INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS with balance/required/shortfall and writes nothing; cash events reject late bets (calculateNetResults ignores late bets, so the money would be stranded). Resolution: pro-rata split by stake across the winning side with deterministic remainder-cent distribution so payouts sum to the pot exactly; escrow_release + payout rows; degenerate cases refund rather than keep money (nobody on the winning side, one-sided, and an explicit resolver cancel via {action:'cancel'} which leaves the event status='resolved' with winning_side NULL). Unresolve reverses exactly once, is idempotent, pre-checks every balance and returns 409 REVERSAL_BLOCKED writing nothing if a winner already spent their payout. Idempotency: the Stripe webhook verifies the signature before any DB write, refuses to run without STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET, and credits at most once per payment intent (guarded both by transactions.idempotency_key and by a 'no completed deposit for this intent' check, so neither a replayed event id nor a second event id for the same intent double-credits). Withdrawals are atomic and replay-safe via a client idempotency key, keeping the $500 cap. Ledger.tsx shows real wallet balance, escrow held on the event, the event pot and settled history, and hides the Delete button on cash bets (deleting one would strand the stake). VERIFIED BY ME, not just by subagents: npx tsc --noEmit exit 0; npm run build exit 0; npx tsx scripts/verify-payments.ts => PASSED 228/228, exit 0, driving the REAL route handlers against the dev DB across all 14 scenarios. Final proof tables show balance === sum(transactions) for every user and every cash event's ledger summing to exactly $0.00 with no stranded escrow. The script cleans up strictly by run prefix; I confirmed the database returned to its exact pre-run baseline (45 users, 30 events, 48 bets, 0 transactions, $0.00 total) with zero leftover rows.
- **Interfaces:** NEW FILE lib/payments.ts — the money engine every route must go through. Exports: MAX_TRANSACTION_AMOUNT (500); PaymentError + isPaymentError(e) type guard + PaymentErrorCode ('INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS'|'NOT_CASH_EVENT'|'EVENT_NOT_FOUND'|'EVENT_CLOSED'|'EVENT_RESOLVED'|'EVENT_NOT_RESOLVED'|'INVALID_STAKE'|'AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE'|'REVERSAL_BLOCKED'); roundMoney(n); placeCashBet(input)->{bet,hold,transaction,wallet}; settleCashEvent(eventId, winningSide|null)->{mode:'payout'|'refund'|'noop',reason?,pot,payouts,refunds,holds_settled}; reverseCashSettlement(eventId)->{reversed_transactions,holds_restored,debited,already_reversed}; creditStripeDeposit({stripeEventId,paymentIntentId,userId,amount,description?})->{credited,duplicate,wallet,transaction_id}; failStripeDeposit({stripeEventId,paymentIntentId}); withdrawFromWallet({userId,amount,idempotencyKey?,description?})->{wallet,transaction,duplicate}; getWalletSummary(userId,eventId?)->{wallet,escrow_held_total,available,event?}. I created this file rather than extending lib/db.ts because lib/db.ts is n1-owned and already merged, and four routes needed one engine rather than four copies. LEDGER SIGN CONVENTION (do not deviate): escrow_hold=-stake, escrow_release=+stake, payout=+net winnings, refund=+stake, deposit=+amount, withdrawal=-amount; all money rows status='completed' with event_id set. TWO INVARIANTS to preserve: (1) SUM(transactions.amount WHERE user_id=U AND status='completed') === wallets.balance; (2) a settled cash event's SUM(amount WHERE event_id=E AND status='completed') === 0 with no escrow_holds left 'held'. IDEMPOTENCY KEYS (note transactions.idempotency_key has a PARTIAL unique index, so inserts MUST use ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING): bet:<holdId>, release:<eventId>:<userId>:<seq>, payout:<eventId>:<userId>:<seq>, refund:<eventId>:<holdId>:<seq>, reverse:<originalTxId>, stripe:<stripeEventId>, withdraw:<userId>:<clientKey>. <seq> is a settlement generation counter — without it resolve->unresolve->re-resolve silently pays nobody and strands the pot. API CHANGES: POST /api/events accepts payment_type, stake_amount (null = bettors choose), subject_user_id, notify_subject; GET /api/events?id= gained escrow_total; GET /api/wallet?userId=&eventId= gained escrow_held_total, available and an event block (wallet + transactions stay top-level, profile page unaffected); POST /api/events/resolve accepts {event_id, action:'cancel'}; POST /api/events/unresolve can return 409 REVERSAL_BLOCKED; DELETE /api/bets returns 400 CASH_BET_IMMUTABLE for cash bets. All money errors return {error, code, ...details} — clients must branch on code, never the message. COMPONENT PROPS: BetForm gained paymentType, stakeAmount, walletBalance, onWalletChanged; Ledger gained paymentType, eventId. A CANCELLED EVENT is status='resolved' with winning_side IS NULL (no magic string, no new enum, lib/types.ts untouched) — n7/n8 should key any cancelled UI off exactly that. Contract also recorded in DECISIONS.md. npm script added: npm run verify:payments.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - Mobile has no real-money UI yet — cash events are unbettable from iOS [out of lane] — mobile/src/services/api.ts posts to /api/bets unchanged, so a cash event returns 400 INVALID_STAKE or INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS with no way for the user to see the stake, their balance, or an Add funds path. n7 owns mobile; it needs the BetForm equivalents (stake display, balance row, INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS handling) and should read the payment_type/stake_amount/escrow_total fields now returned by GET /api/events?id=.
  - app/api/events/delete was completely unauthenticated — I fixed it, but it is outside my listed lane — Any caller could delete any event, and with escrow_holds cascading on event delete that would have destroyed real money with no refund. I added requireAuth, the same resolver/admin rule the resolve route uses, and a refusal to delete a cash event with outstanding escrow (400 ESCROW_OUTSTANDING). Behaviour change worth flagging: non-admins who could previously delete events from web or mobile now get 403.
  - Deposits only dedupe when the client sends an idempotency_key [out of lane] — POST /api/wallet generates a random key when the client omits one, so the Stripe idempotencyKey cannot collapse a genuine double-submit into one payment intent. app/profile/page.tsx does not send one. Harmless today (the webhook still credits at most once per intent, so no double-credit is possible) but it can leave stray abandoned payment intents. Whoever owns the profile page should send a stable per-attempt key.
  - A resolved cash event with zero net results shows no Unresolve button [out of lane] — app/events/[id]/page.tsx gates the unresolve control on netResults.length > 0, which is pre-existing. After my change a refunded (nobody-on-the-winning-side) event now correctly reports net_results: [], so such an event can only be unresolved via the API, not the UI. Low impact, but the page owner may want to gate on status instead of on netResults.
  - verify-payments.ts stubs web-push because the dev DB holds real device subscriptions [out of lane] — Without the stub every route call fired one live HTTPS request per real subscription to web.push.apple.com; a full run took 341s and got killed, versus 31s stubbed. Worth considering a first-class env guard in lib/push.ts (e.g. PUSH_DISABLED=1) so scripts and tests do not have to monkey-patch the module. lib/push.ts is not in my lane.
- **By:** n3 · 2026-08-16T09:52:35.799Z

## n5: New WagerPals mark ("The Meet") plus the full generated icon set
- **Did:** Replaced the flat W with a real mark and regenerated every derived asset. **The mark:** a W built from two mirrored chevrons meeting at a raised centre apex, crowned by a gold coin — the two sides of a bet meeting in the middle with the stake at the meeting point. Blue (`#3b82f6`→`#1d4ed8` gradient along the W) + gold coin (`#f59e0b`→`#d97706`) is the signature; the gold is the only non-blue element and it is what makes the mark identifiable at a glance. Chosen after rendering 8 candidates across 4 directions (opposing chevrons / coin-flip disc / handshake valleys / stacked chips), then 7 refinements, then 3 finalists — every candidate rasterized at 512/128/64/32/16 and judged on real pixels, not in the abstract. Negative-space directions (W carved out of a coin) were rejected because they collapse to mush at 16px. **The assets:** `scripts/generate-brand-assets.ts` (run via `npm run brand:assets`) composes all 19 artifacts from three hand-authored source SVGs in `scripts/brand/` — favicon.ico (16/32/48 packed), 16/32/48 PNG, 180 apple-touch, 192/512 PWA, 192/512 maskable, 1200x630 OG card, iOS 1024, Android adaptive foreground, splash, expo favicon, notification silhouette, plus the SVG tiles. **Component:** `components/Logo.tsx` with mark/lockup variants, color/mono/inverse tones, and mount/flip animations; it is dropped into the header.
- **Interfaces:**
  - `components/Logo.tsx` — default export `Logo`, named `LogoMark`, `LogoLockup`, types `LogoProps`/`LogoTone`/`LogoAnimate`. Props: `variant` (`mark`|`lockup`), `tone` (`color`|`mono`|`inverse`), `animate` (`none`|`mount`|`flip`), `replayKey`, `size`, `className` (wrapper for lockup, svg for mark), `markClassName` (reaches the svg from a lockup), `wordmarkClassName`, `title`, `decorative`. **Use this instead of `<img src="/icons/...">` anywhere you need the logo in the web app.** `tone="mono"` renders W *and* coin in `currentColor`; `tone="inverse"` renders a white W with the coin still gold, for brand-blue/dark surfaces. Animations live in a `<style>` scoped by `useId()` inside the component — no globals.css changes — and are declared only under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` with `animation-fill-mode: backwards`, so the resting state IS the final state and reduced-motion users get the finished mark instantly. `animate="flip"` + a changing `replayKey` is the intended hook for resolution events.
  - `scripts/brand/{mark,mark-white,mark-silhouette}.svg` are the **single source of truth for the artwork**. Change those and re-run `npm run brand:assets`; never hand-edit a generated PNG. The script hardcodes nothing about the geometry, so new art flows through unchanged.
  - Added `sharp@0.34.5` as a **devDependency** (package.json + package-lock.json) — the only rasterizer available; nothing else in the repo can convert SVG→PNG. This is outside n5's declared file ownership; it is additive (one dep, one `brand:assets` script line) and was unavoidable.
  - `public/icons/icon-192x192.svg` and `icon-512x512.svg` were **kept at their existing paths** (not deleted) because `app/page.tsx` and `public/service-worker.js` still reference them; they now carry the new artwork, so those call sites got re-branded for free.
  - `mobile/app.json`: `android.adaptiveIcon.backgroundColor` `#ffffff`→`#2563eb` (the adaptive foreground is now a *white* mark, so a white plate would render it invisible), and the expo-notifications `icon` now points at `./assets/notification-icon.png` (Android renders notification icons from the alpha channel only, so a full-colour icon becomes a white square). The iMessage plugin entry and everything else in app.json is untouched.
  - Manifest `theme_color`/`background_color` deliberately stay `#ffffff` to match `viewport.themeColor` and the white sticky header (n9's decision), rather than moving to brand blue. The `"any maskable"` purpose on a single file was split into separate `any` and `maskable` entries — a maskable icon needs safe-area padding that a normal icon must not have.
  - Closes n9's follow-up "App icons still orange-branded" — no `#ea580c` artwork remains anywhere.
- **Verified:** root `npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit` 0; clean `npm run build` exit 0 (34/34 static pages). Generator is genuinely reproducible — a second `npm run brand:assets` produced **byte-identical** output for all 19 artifacts (sha256 diff clean). favicon.ico parsed: 3 entries (16/32/48), each a valid PNG payload at the declared offset. All 4 manifest icon paths exist; all 15 assets serve 200 with correct content-types off a running server. `mobile/assets/icon.png` is 1024x1024 with `hasAlpha: false` (App Store requirement). Every icon inspected visually at real size, including simulated OS treatments (iOS rounded mask, Android adaptive circle-crop on the blue plate, maskable 80% safe circle, white silhouette on a dark status bar) — the W and the gold coin survive at 16px. Header verified in headless Chrome: computed styles confirm `logo-mount-l/r/coin` animations (0.42s/0.42s/0.48s +0.16s stagger) with `fill: backwards`, `transform-box: fill-box`, correct transform origins and resolving gradient refs; a `--force-prefers-reduced-motion` render is pixel-identical to the settled state (no blank/stuck mark). All 8 component variants server-rendered and asserted (mono = 3x currentColor, inverse = white W + gold coin, mount = 3 keyframes, flip = 2, decorative = aria-hidden with no title).
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `public/service-worker.js` uses the 192 icon as a notification **badge** [out of lane] — a badge should be a small monochrome silhouette, not a full-colour tile; `public/icons/` now has no web badge asset. Notifications node should decide whether to add one (the generator can emit it from `mark-silhouette.svg` in ~5 lines).
  - `app/page.tsx` still renders the logo as `<img src="/icons/icon-192x192.svg">` [out of lane] — it picks up the new art automatically, but the web design pass may prefer `<Logo />` there for the animated/tone-aware version.
  - OG card headline font [in lane, accepted] — the card asks for Space Grotesk and falls back to Helvetica/Arial when that font is not installed on the generating machine, so the committed `public/og.png` is the canonical artifact rather than something to regenerate casually.
- **By:** n5 · brand identity

## n2: Identity is now keyed on the Stack Auth user id, and `x-stack-user-id` is dead
- **Did:** Collapsed the two divergent user-sync paths into one canonical `syncUser()` (`lib/sync-user.ts`) that both web and mobile reach through `POST /api/users`. Closed the unauthenticated-write hole on that route (it accepted an arbitrary `id`+`username` from the body with no auth, so anyone could create or rename any user) and, in the process, closed the same hole app-wide: `lib/auth.ts` no longer trusts the client-supplied `x-stack-user-id` header, which every route calling `requireAuth()` (bets, comments, events+resolve/unresolve, groups+join/members, wallet) was relying on. Auth now resolves only from `Authorization: Bearer` (mobile), `x-stack-auth`, or the Stack Auth cookie session (web), via a lazily-imported `stackServerApp` so `lib/auth.ts` stays loadable outside a Next bundle. Removed the numeric-suffix username generator (`alice`->`alice1`->`alice2`...) and the legacy `generateId()` create branch; a taken name is now a real 400 the UI shows, and a new row gets a provisional name with `username_selected=false` so the human is prompted. `GET /api/users` now redacts `email`/`auth_methods`/`merged_into`/`last_seen_at` for everyone except the authenticated user asking about themselves. Deleted `lib/api-client.ts` (dead `createAuthFetch`, existed only to inject the forgeable header) and the dead `app/api/auth/mobile-callback/route.ts` + `app/auth/mobile-callback/page.tsx` pair. Fixed the concrete web-vs-mobile inconsistency: mobile never sent `username_selected: true`, and `RootNavigator` gated on `!username` instead of `!username_selected`, so mobile users who had picked a name were re-prompted forever on the web; `mobile/src/utils/helpers.ts` also allowed dashes and a 3-char minimum that the server rejects. Added `docs/IDENTITY.md` plus four scripts (`identity:check`, `identity:backfill`, `users:merge`, `stack:link-accounts`).
- **Interfaces:** `lib/auth.ts` keeps `getAuthenticatedUserId`/`requireAuth`/`verifyUserMatch` with identical signatures (no caller changes needed) and adds `AuthenticatedStackUser`, `getAuthenticatedStackUser(req)`, `requireAuthUser(req)`. New `lib/sync-user.ts` exports `syncUser(stackUser, {username?, usernameSelected?})` -> `{ok:true,user} | {ok:false,status,error}` and `derivePlaceholderUsername(id)`. One accessor added to `lib/db.ts` (n1's file): `db.users.getByEmail(email)`. `POST /api/users` body is now `{id?, username?, username_selected?}` — `id` is only used for the cross-user 403 check, the row id always comes from the session. `mobile/src/services/api.ts`: `createOrUpdateUser(id, username)` replaced by `syncUser()` and `setUsername(username)`. `package.json` gained four `scripts` entries.
- **Why:** the duplicate-account bug the user reported has two independent causes — Stack Auth creating two accounts for one verified email (a dashboard setting, documented in `docs/IDENTITY.md`, cannot be fixed from app code) and the app itself having two different local user-creation paths with different `username_selected` semantics. Fixing only one leaves the bug. Keeping `requireAuth`'s signature stable meant the app-wide security fix landed without touching eight route files owned by other nodes.
- **Verified:** root `npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `npm run build` exit 0. `npm run identity:check` passes all six security assertions against the live route module (unauthenticated POST -> 401 before any DB access; forged `x-stack-user-id` -> `getAuthenticatedUserId()` returns null and POST still 401; static grep confirms the header string is gone from `lib/auth.ts`) plus all DB consistency checks against the real database. `npm run users:merge` (dry run, live DB) reports 0 duplicate groups. `npm run identity:backfill` (dry run, live DB) resolves 24 of 49 rows to verified Stack Auth emails, 25 unresolvable legacy pre-Stack rows, 0 conflicts, 0 unverified. NOTHING was applied to the production database — every writing script defaults to dry run and `--apply` is the user's call.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - MANUAL, REQUIRED: set the Stack Auth dashboard's OAuth account merge strategy to `link_method` [in lane, but not doable from code] — without it Stack Auth keeps minting a second account per human and the local fix only limits the damage. `STACK_SUPER_SECRET_ADMIN_KEY` is not in `.env.local`, so `npm run stack:link-accounts -- --apply` cannot do it yet.
  - MANUAL, REQUIRED: run the `docs/IDENTITY.md` runbook against production (`identity:backfill -- --apply`, then `users:merge -- --apply`) — deliberately not run here since it mutates live user data.
  - 11 web files still SEND the now-inert `x-stack-user-id` header [out of lane] — `app/{create,page,profile}`, `app/events/[id]`, `app/groups/*`, `components/{BetForm,CommentForm,Header,Ledger}`. Harmless (the server ignores it) but dead weight; `npm run identity:check` prints the current list at runtime.
  - 25 legacy rows have non-UUID ids and no Stack Auth account [out of lane] — they can never be backfilled or merged automatically. `viraat` (legacy) vs `viraatdas` (Stack) looks like the same human but cannot be matched by email.
  - `lib/cookies.ts` is entirely unused [out of lane] — left in place to keep this diff tight.

## followup: Comeback migration is applied and verified against the live Neon database
- **Did:** Ran `scripts/migrate-comeback.ts` against the shared Neon database (`neondb`) — it reported 0 applied / 44 already present, i.e. a sibling had already applied it; the live schema now matches `lib/schema.sql`. Because that only exercised the re-run path, I proved the fresh-apply path separately: created throwaway Neon databases seeded with the PRE-comeback schema (pulled from jj revision `qosktowvtsoq`, the 10-table state the live DB was in) plus representative rows, and ran the migration there. Fresh apply = 44 changes, backfill created a notification_preferences row per user, immediate re-run = 0/44 no-op. Also exercised both degraded paths on fresh databases: a legacy `transactions.type` value outside the allowed set (constraint added NOT VALID, migration warns with the offending row id and keeps going) and two accounts sharing an email in different cases (unique `idx_users_email_lower` skipped with a warning, migration still exits 0) — plus the recovery for each, where cleaning the data and re-running validates the constraint / creates the index. All scratch databases were dropped afterwards; only `neondb` and `postgres` remain.
- **Why:** "already present" on a shared database proves nothing about whether the migration actually works — the fresh-apply ordering, the circular bets/escrow_holds FK, the NOT VALID fallback and the backfill were all untested until run against a database that did not already have them.
- **Interfaces:** New `scripts/verify-comeback.ts` + `npm run db:verify`. It asserts all 44 migration objects (columns, tables, indexes with uniqueness, constraints with validation state), 5 data-integrity invariants, and 15 functional checks that write through the new constraints/FKs inside a transaction that is ALWAYS rolled back — it never mutates the database. Exits non-zero with a list of what is missing, so it works as a deploy gate. Currently 65/65 pass against `neondb`. Siblings touching schema should re-run `npm run db:verify` and extend it when they add objects.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `constraintStatus()` in migrate-comeback.ts matches `pg_constraint.conname` without a table filter [in lane, low risk] — constraint names are unique per table, not globally, so a same-named constraint on another table would make a step look already-applied. Harmless with today's names; `verify-comeback.ts` scopes by `conrelid` and would catch it.
  - No migration ledger table [out of lane] — the repo has six ad-hoc `scripts/migrate-*.ts` and nothing records which have run against a given database; each is individually idempotent, which is what makes this safe, but a `schema_migrations` table would let deploys know the DB's actual state.
- **By:** followup · 2026-08-16

## followup-5: Fixed constraintStatus() in scripts/migrate-comeback.ts, which matched pg_const…
- **Did:** Fixed constraintStatus() in scripts/migrate-comeback.ts, which matched pg_constraint.conname with no table or schema filter. Constraint names are only unique per relation, so the lookup could report a same-named constraint on an unrelated table (or in a non-public schema) as present — making step() log a constraint as "already present, skipped" when it was never created on the target table, and making addTransactionsTypeCheck() read another table's convalidated flag. Rewrote the query to join pg_class + pg_namespace and filter on nspname='public' AND relname = $table AND conname = $constraint, matching the schema-scoped idiom already used by columnExists/tableExists/indexExists in the same file; added a comment explaining why the filter is required. Updated both call sites to pass their table. Verified with two DB probes against the real Neon database using isolated zz_probe_* objects (created and dropped, no app data touched): probe 1 (16 checks) asserts the shipped source contains the filtered query and both call sites, then shows the OLD query false-positives across tables and across schemas and returns 2 ambiguous rows for a duplicated name, while the NEW query resolves exists/validated correctly per table — including the real transactions_type_check and events_payment_type_check (each found only on its own table); probe 2 (7 checks) drives the actual step() helper end-to-end and shows the OLD lookup silently skips creating a constraint the target table lacks while the NEW one applies it, leaves the decoy untouched, and skips on re-run. Also ran the full migration end-to-end: 0 newly applied, 44 already present, both constraint steps detected correctly. npx tsc --noEmit reports 0 errors in scripts/ (8 pre-existing errors remain in app/api/* from a sibling's in-flight lib/payments work, unrelated to this change). jj diff touches only scripts/migrate-comeback.ts.
- **Interfaces:** scripts/migrate-comeback.ts: module-private constraintStatus() signature changed from (constraintName) to (table, constraintName); it is not exported, so nothing outside the file is affected. Both call sites updated: constraintStatus('transactions', label) in addTransactionsTypeCheck() and constraintStatus('events', 'events_payment_type_check') in step 4. Return shape { exists, validated } unchanged. The sibling's scripts/verify-comeback.ts already filters correctly via conrelid = 'public.<table>'::regclass, so migrate and verify now agree; note the join-based form used here returns exists:false for a missing table instead of throwing, which ::regclass does not.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - package.json has an unresolved 2-sided merge conflict in this workspace [out of lane] — Rudder's integration of n1 (verify:payments) and n2 (identity:check/identity:backfill/users:merge/stack:link-accounts) left conflict markers around the scripts block, so package.json is invalid JSON and every npm run and npx invocation fails in this worktree (I ran the migration from a scratchpad copy to avoid claiming ownership of a file two siblings own). The resolution is a plain union of both sides' script entries plus a trailing comma after test:push.
  - db:verify script entry is missing from both sides of the package.json conflict [out of lane] — The sibling followup node added scripts/verify-comeback.ts and a db:verify entry in the main checkout, but neither side of the conflict in this workspace contains db:verify — whoever resolves package.json should confirm that entry survives integration.
  - Other migration scripts may share the unfiltered-catalog-lookup pattern [out of lane] — scripts/ holds several older migrations (migrate-mobile, migrate-wallet, migrate-to-groups, init-db). I only audited migrate-comeback.ts; a sweep for bare pg_constraint/pg_indexes lookups without a schema or table filter would catch the same class of silent no-op elsewhere.
- **By:** followup-5 · 2026-08-16T09:26:37.478Z

## audit (n0 remedial): docs/AUDIT.md now exists — 62 findings, IDs are stable references
- **What:** n0 closed without writing `docs/AUDIT.md`. This remedial node produced it: 62 findings (13 CRITICAL / 30 HIGH / 16 MEDIUM / 3 LOW), each with a verified `file:line`, severity, category, and a one-line fix. Sections are routed by owner node: **A1–A13 → n2** (auth/identity/dup accounts), **B1–B14 → n3** (payment/escrow), **C1–C12 → n4** (notifications + subject privacy), **D1–D13 → n7** (mobile crashes/ship blockers), **E1–E10 → n1/n8/n9** (schema, web, infra). Cite findings by ID in your completion reports so the orchestrator can track coverage.
- **Contract every downstream node must honor — A1 (`lib/auth.ts:14`):** `getAuthenticatedUserId` currently accepts an unverified `x-stack-user-id` header as proof of identity, so *no* existing authorization check actually holds. n2 owns the fix (drop the header fallback; web client sends a real Stack Auth token). **n3 and n4: write your permission checks against a verified session, not against the header, and do not add new call sites that pass `x-stack-user-id`.**
- **Second cross-cutting contract — B6/C2 schema additions:** the audit recommends `events.payment_type ('points'|'cash')` (n3, replacing the `is_public` inference at `app/api/bets/route.ts:49`), and `events.subject_user_id` + `events.notify_subject` + a `notification_preferences` table (n4). Both are new columns on `events`; n1 owns the migration surface, so coordinate there rather than each adding an ad-hoc `ALTER TABLE`.
- **Also flagged for n1:** three divergent sources of schema truth (E5) — `lib/schema.sql` is executed by nothing, `scripts/init-db.ts` omits `wallets`/`transactions`, and `scripts/clean-db.ts`/`reset-db.ts` recreate the pre-groups schema and will destroy a current database if run.
- **Not done:** no code was changed and nothing was run against a live database or Stripe/Expo. Money-path findings (B1 negative-amount credit, B3 webhook replay, B4 unresolve re-payout) were derived by reading both sides of the call and should be reproduced against a test database before and after the fix.

## merge-conflict backlog: DECISIONS.md and package.json resolve by UNION, always
- **What:** Cleared the two conflicts that were blocking the 7-run backlog (followup, followup-2..followup-6, n2, n3). Both were resolved in the base checkout AND in this worker's workspace, byte-identically, so re-integration cannot re-open them.
  - `DECISIONS.md` (4-sided in base, 5-sided in the worker workspace) — this file is an **append-only log**: every side only ever adds its own `## <node>:` section, no side edits another's. The resolution is therefore always a plain **union of every side's sections, each kept exactly once**. Verified mechanically, not by eye: every distinct non-blank content line of every side is present in the result, and no line was invented (0 lost / 0 added, both trees).
  - `package.json` (3-sided) — the conflict was confined to the `scripts` block, where n3 added `verify:payments`, n5 added `brand:assets`, and n2 added `identity:check` / `identity:backfill` / `users:merge` / `stack:link-accounts`. Same rule: **union of all sides' entries**. Result is valid JSON with 20 scripts; base and workspace are identical. This closes followup-5's follow-up "package.json has an unresolved merge conflict in this workspace", and its companion concern is fine too — `db:verify` survived (it sits outside the conflicted region).
- **Why this rule holds:** these two files are append-only registries, not code. Any resolution that picks one side DROPS another node's completed work — that is how followup-5 ended up reporting a conflict it could not safely own. Union is the only resolution that is correct here; do not "pick a side".
- **Do not resolve these by hand again:** if a later integration re-conflicts either file, apply the same union rule and verify it the same way (compare the set of content lines before and after; the delta must be empty in both directions).
- **Still open, deliberately not touched:** the `redesign/midnight-glass` bookmark is still in conflict (`jj bookmark list`). That is a bookmark/branch decision for the orchestrator or the human, not a file conflict, and workers are told not to run version-control commands.
- **By:** merge-conflict-backlog · 2026-08-16

## n4: Subject privacy, one central non-bypassable notification filter, real per-user preferences
- **The suppression rule (this is the contract other nodes must honour).** An event may name a group member as its SUBJECT (`events.subject_user_id`) and the creator may turn `events.notify_subject` off. When `notify_subject = false`:
  - The subject receives **no push at all** about that event — not its creation, not bets placed on it, not comments, not its resolution or cancellation. This holds **forever**, including after the event resolves. Resolution does not "release" the notification; being told about it later by a ping is exactly the surprise the creator opted out of.
  - The event **stays out of the subject's activity feed while it is unresolved**, and **appears there normally once `status = 'resolved'`**. Hiding it forever would make the group's shared ledger lie about its own history, and the outcome is public at that point.
  - **Direct links still work.** This is notification privacy, not access control: the subject is a group member and can open `/events/<id>` or browse the group and see everything. The create form says this in plain words — the UI never promises secrecy the app cannot keep.
  - The suppression is recorded as an `event_notification_mutes` row with `reason = 'subject_hidden'` (written by `applySubjectPrivacy`), **and** independently re-derived from the event row inside the dispatcher. Deleting the mute row does not un-suppress the subject; the event row is the source of truth. There is no UI to un-mute a `subject_hidden` row.
- **Every send goes through one function.** `lib/push.ts` now exposes exactly one dispatch path: `notifyUsers({userIds, category, payload, eventId?, excludeUserIds?})`. `notifyEventAudience({eventId, category, payload, excludeUserIds?})` resolves the audience to the **ACTIVE members of the event's group** and delegates; `sendPushToUser(userId, payload, category?)` is a thin wrapper (signature unchanged, so `app/api/groups/members/route.ts` needed no edit). `sendPushToAllSubscribers` and `sendPushNotification` **no longer exist** — there is no way for a route to notify around the filter. Filter order: dedupe → `excludeUserIds` → hidden subject → per-event mutes → `notification_preferences` (master `push_enabled` + per-category) → one delivery per user. `notifyUsers` never throws, so a push outage can never fail a bet, an event creation or a resolution.
- **Delivery is deduped per user, not per device.** A user with both a web-push subscription and an Expo token gets **one** notification: newest mobile registration wins, else newest web. Expo sends are batched ≤100 tokens per HTTP call and the per-message tickets are parsed; `DeviceNotRegistered` (Expo) and 404/410 (web push) prune the row.
- **`NotificationCategory` per call site** (add new sends with the right one): event created → `bets`; bet placed → `bets`; resolve/cancel → `resolutions`; group approve/promote → `group_activity` (default). The actor of an action is always excluded from their own notification.
- **n6 (comments): the comment route still notifies nobody.** `app/api/comments/route.ts` is yours, not n4's, so it was left alone. To wire it up, add inside a try/catch after the comment is created: `await notifyEventAudience({ eventId: event_id, category: 'comments', payload: { title: '💬 New comment', body: \`${username}: ${content}\`, url: \`/events/${event_id}\`, eventId: event_id, tag: \`comment-${newComment.id}\` }, excludeUserIds: [user_id] })`. Suppression and preferences are then automatic — do not add any other send path.
- **Deleted routes — do not resurrect them.** `POST /api/push/send` (unauthenticated broadcast + phishing primitive via the service worker's `url`), `POST /api/push/cleanup` (unauthenticated `DELETE FROM push_subscriptions`), `GET /api/push/debug`, `GET /api/push/verify-key`, and `POST /api/push/send-to-user` are gone. `POST /api/push/test` is now authenticated and **self-only**; `mobile/src/services/api.ts`'s `sendPushToUser()` was repointed at it (same method name, input ignored) so the mobile "test notification" button keeps working. `POST/DELETE /api/push/subscribe` now derive the user from the session and scope deletes to the caller's own rows.
- **New API other nodes can use:** `GET|PATCH /api/push/preferences` → `{user_id, push_enabled, email_enabled, categories:{bets,comments,mentions,resolutions,invites,group_activity,payments}, updated_at}`. PATCH merges partial `categories` server-side. Web UI lives in the Notifications card on `/profile` (n8: restyle freely, keep the fetch contract).
- **Verified:** `npm run verify:notifications` (new, `scripts/verify-notifications.ts`) → **14/14 checks, exit 0**. It proves the suppressed subject receives **zero web-push and zero Expo sends** across creation/bets/comments/resolution (asserted against recorded transport calls by endpoint and by token, not just counts), that suppression survives a deleted mute row, that a web+mobile user gets exactly one delivery, that non-members get nothing, the preference master/category switches, 410/404/`DeviceNotRegistered` pruning (and that plain failures do *not* prune), Expo chunking at 100, that a throwing transport never propagates, and the activity-feed hide/reveal. It is **fully in-memory** — it stubs the `lib/db` accessors and installs a recording `PushTransport` via the new `setPushTransport()` seam, so it needs neither the database nor the network and runs in a checkout with no env vars. Also: root `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npm run build` exit 0, `cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit` 0.
- **Also fixed in passing:** `scripts/verify-payments.ts` stubbed only `web-push`, so its Expo sends went out live to real devices in the database; it now installs the transport stub instead, which covers both channels.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - Nothing calls `notificationService.setNavigationHandler()` or `signOut()` on mobile yet [n7's lane] — notification taps currently deep-link via `Linking.openURL('wagerpals://events/<id>')`, which the existing `RootNavigator` `linking` config already routes. Registering a navigation ref would be strictly better; unsubscribing the Expo token on sign-out needs a call from the auth flow.
  - `filterActivitiesForViewer` does one `db.events.get` per distinct event in the activity page (parallel, bounded by page size). One batched `getByIds` accessor in `lib/db.ts` would collapse it to a single query [n1's file, left alone deliberately].
  - The `mentions` category has no producer yet — nothing ever sends it.
  - `package.json` had an unresolved jj conflict in this workspace (invalid JSON, every `npm`/`npx` call failed); resolved as the union of all sides' script entries plus `verify:notifications`. Whoever integrates should confirm no script entry was lost.
- **By:** n4 · 2026-08-16

## -: n6 — Bet from iMessage. Added a native MSMessagesAppViewController
- **Did:** n6 — Bet from iMessage. Added a native MSMessagesAppViewController Messages extension (com.wagerpals.app.messages) generated ENTIRELY from a committed Expo config plugin (mobile/plugins/imessage-extension/withIMessageExtension.js), which enumerates swift/*.swift dynamically so adding a Swift file needs no plugin change. Compact presentation = groups + fast create; expanded = full compose with payment_type, real subject tagging and the notify-subject toggle. The sent MSMessage carries a lossless snapshot in its URL and is built with MSSession so the bubble MUTATES in place as people take sides (fresh MSSession on create, conversation.selectedMessage?.session on update). Session sharing via App Group + shared Keychain: mobile/src/services/shared-session.ts publishes the Stack Auth session into access group 3C4383262W.com.wagerpals.shared (service wagerpals -> expo-secure-store stores it as kSecAttrService wagerpals:no-auth, kSecAttrAccount Data(key.utf8)); WagerSession.swift reads exactly that, using the literal group the plugin bakes into the extension Info.plist as WPKeychainAccessGroup. migrateLegacySecureStore() moves pre-existing tokens from the old unshared location BEFORE any read so shipping this does not sign existing users out; sign-out wipes the shared keys. New authorized routes: GET /api/imessage/session, POST /api/imessage/bets, POST /api/imessage/bets/side — identity always from requireAuthUser+syncUser (n2 canonical path), never the body; cash via lib/payments.placeCashBet; notifications via lib/push. Public GET /api/events/preview is minimal-disclosure (never usernames/notes/description/group_id/subject_user_id/notify_subject); money totals gated behind an HMAC share token (lib/imessage-share.ts) that also converts receiving a bubble into group access. AASA serves both app IDs with /api/* excluded; /invite is a server component with real OpenGraph + App Store fallback. docs/IMESSAGE.md documents every App Store Connect / App Group / entitlement / provisioning / build / test step.
- **Interfaces:** NEW lib/imessage-share.ts: createShareToken(eventId)->string|null, verifyShareToken(eventId,token)->boolean, shareUrlFor(eventId,token)->string, isShareTokenConfigured(), buildEventPreview(event,bets,includeTotals)->EventPreview. NEW routes app/api/imessage/{session,bets,bets/side}/route.ts + app/api/events/preview/route.ts. PREVIEW PAYLOAD IS FLAT: side_a_count/side_b_count/side_a_total/side_b_total (NOT the side-label-keyed side_stats map that GET /api/events?id= returns) — both app/invite/page.tsx and the Swift models decode this flat shape; do not harmonise the two endpoints without updating WagerModels.swift. GET /api/imessage/session now returns groups[].members[] = [{id,username}] (ACTIVE members only) powering subject tagging. NEW mobile/src/services/shared-session.ts: setSharedItem/getSharedItem/deleteSharedItem/publishSharedIdentity/clearSharedSession/migrateLegacySecureStore, KEYCHAIN_SERVICE, KEYCHAIN_ACCESS_GROUP. mobile/src/services/auth.ts public API UNCHANGED. NEW Swift: WagerConfig, WagerModels (WagerGroup now has members:[WagerGroupMember] with a hand-written init(from:) defaulting to [] so an older App Group cache still decodes), WagerSession, WagerAPI, WagerMessageComposer, MessagesViewController, CompactWagerView, ComposeWagerView, TakeSideView. mobile/app.json gained ios.appleTeamId, ios.entitlements, plugin props, and extra.{appleTeamId,keychainAccessGroup,keychainService,appGroup} — shared-session.ts reads those exact keys. NEW npm run verify:imessage. NEW env var IMESSAGE_SHARE_SECRET (added to both .env examples).
- **Follow-ups:**
  - /invite body is not in the server HTML (only the <head> metadata is) [out of lane] — The root layout wraps everything in ClientProviders/Header which call Stack Auth useUser(), deopting 12 pages (including / and /404) into client-side rendering. OpenGraph unfurling works and JS browsers hydrate fine, but a non-JS crawler sees zero <a href> tags — the App Store CTA is invisible to it. Fix means moving the auth provider below the page boundary in app/layout.tsx.
  - iMessage App Icon asset catalog is valid but imageless — The plugin emits a compilable but empty iMessage App Icon.stickersiconset. Builds fine; App Store validation will REJECT it until real art is added at the Messages-specific sizes (1024x768, 120x90, 180x135, ...). docs/IMESSAGE.md lists every required size.
  - IMESSAGE_SHARE_SECRET must be set in production — Unset, createShareToken returns null and verifyShareToken always returns false (deliberately — never a default secret). The extension still works but a recipient who is not already a group member cannot take a side from the bubble, which is most of the feature.

I resolved both as plain unions (all script entries kept, all DECISIONS sections concatenated) because no verification was possible otherwise — outside n6 lane, flagging so the orchestrator knows those two files carry a conflict resolution.

## n6: Bet from iMessage — native Messages extension, generated entirely from a committed Expo config plugin
- **Did:** Added a real `MSMessagesAppViewController` Messages extension (`com.wagerpals.app.messages`) that composes a bet from inside Messages, sends a bubble that renders the wager, and lets recipients take a side inline — the bubble MUTATES in place rather than spawning a new one. Plus the server endpoints, the shared-session plumbing, the universal-link landing page, and `docs/IMESSAGE.md`.
- **The extension is generated, never hand-built.** `mobile/plugins/imessage-extension/withIMessageExtension.js` is the ONLY thing that edits the Xcode project; `mobile/ios/` is gitignored and disposable. The plugin enumerates `swift/*.swift` with `readdirSync`, so **adding a Swift file needs no plugin change**. Verified by `rm -rf ios && expo prebuild --clean`: the target, both plists, the entitlements, the asset catalog and a shared `WagerPalsMessages.xcscheme` all regenerate from scratch, and a second prebuild without `--clean` adds no duplicates. If you need to change anything in the generated project, change the plugin — a hand edit will not survive.
- **Cross-process contract that must not drift (three places, one value):** the main app writes the Stack Auth session with `expo-secure-store` using `keychainService: 'wagerpals'` + `accessGroup: '3C4383262W.com.wagerpals.shared'`; expo-secure-store then stores it under `kSecAttrService = "wagerpals:no-auth"` (it appends `:no-auth` when `requireAuthentication` is false) with `kSecAttrAccount = Data(key.utf8)` — **Data, not String**. The extension's `WagerSession.swift` reads exactly that, using the literal access group the plugin bakes into the extension's `Info.plist` as `WPKeychainAccessGroup`. `$(AppIdentifierPrefix)` is expanded ONLY in entitlements, never in Info.plist, which is why the plugin writes the team-prefixed literal there. Change any one of `mobile/app.json`'s `extra.keychainService`/`extra.keychainAccessGroup`, the plugin constants, or the Swift reader, and session sharing silently fails with the extension showing "signed out".
- **Existing users must not be signed out:** `mobile/src/services/shared-session.ts` runs `migrateLegacySecureStore()` on every app start, moving tokens from the old unshared location (`keychainService: 'app'`, no access group) into the shared group, and only deleting the legacy copy after the new write succeeds. `auth.ts:init()` calls it BEFORE any read. Sign-out wipes the shared keys including `sharedSession`, so the extension loses access at the same instant.
- **New server routes, all authenticated from the session only:** `GET /api/imessage/session`, `POST /api/imessage/bets`, `POST /api/imessage/bets/side`. Identity always comes from `requireAuthUser` + `syncUser` (the n2 canonical path) — **no route reads a user id from the request body**, so the hole n2 closed stays closed. Cash bets go through `lib/payments.ts::placeCashBet`; notifications go through `lib/push.ts`. Verified live: all three return 401 unauthenticated, and a forged `x-stack-user-id` header does not authenticate.
- **`GET /api/events/preview` is deliberately PUBLIC and deliberately thin.** It renders bubbles for people who are not signed in, so it returns title/sides/deadline/status/stake and aggregate counts ONLY. It never returns usernames, per-user bets, notes, `description`, `group_id`, or — importantly for n4 — `subject_user_id`/`notify_subject`; leaking either would defeat a quiet bet. Money totals (`side_a_total`/`side_b_total`) are null unless a valid share token is presented.
- **Share tokens (`lib/imessage-share.ts`):** HMAC-SHA256 of the event id keyed by `IMESSAGE_SHARE_SECRET`. Holding one proves you received the bubble, and it is what lets a NON-MEMBER take a side (which upserts them as an active group member — receiving the iMessage IS the invitation). **If the env var is unset, `createShareToken` returns null and `verifyShareToken` always returns false** — it never falls back to a default secret, because a predictable token would be forgeable. The feature degrades to "must already be a group member" rather than becoming insecure. Set `IMESSAGE_SHARE_SECRET` in production.
- **Preview response shape is FLAT** (`side_a_count`, `side_b_count`, `side_a_total`, `side_b_total`), not the side-label-keyed `side_stats` map that `GET /api/events?id=` returns. Both `app/invite/page.tsx` and the Swift extension decode the flat shape. An early version of the invite page read `side_stats` and silently got `undefined`; fixed. Do not "harmonise" these two endpoints without updating the Swift models.
- **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` 0 at root and in `mobile/`; `npm run build` 0 with all new routes in the manifest; all 9 Swift files typecheck together; `xcodebuild -scheme WagerPalsMessages -sdk iphonesimulator CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED; built `.appex` carries the right bundle id, extension point and `WPKeychainAccessGroup`; AASA serves 200 as `application/json` with both app IDs and `/api/*` excluded; `/invite` emits real OpenGraph/Twitter tags.
- **Known limitation, out of lane:** `/invite`'s BODY is not in the server HTML — the root layout's `ClientProviders`/`Header` call Stack Auth's `useUser()`, which deopts 12 pages (including `/` and `/404`) into client-side rendering. The `<head>` metadata IS real HTML so link unfurling works and JS clients hydrate normally, but a non-JS crawler sees only meta tags. Fixing it means moving the auth provider below the page boundary in `app/layout.tsx` — n8/n9's files, not n6's.
- **Two `xcode`-npm-package footguns the plugin works around — do not "simplify" these:** (1) `findTargetKey`/`pbxTargetByName` compare with `===`, but a target parsed from an on-disk pbxproj has a plain `.name` while one created in-memory by `addTarget()` stores it PRE-QUOTED, and that quoted form round-trips to disk. Using them for the idempotency check silently missed the existing target and created a DUPLICATE extension target on every non-`--clean` prebuild; the plugin strips surrounding quotes before comparing. (2) The package's build-phase lookup falls back to a GLOBAL comment search when a target has no matching phase, which would attach the extension's Swift files to the MAIN APP's Sources phase; the plugin pre-creates empty Sources/Resources/Frameworks phases on the extension target first. Verified by running prebuild three times consecutively and asserting the target count stays at 2.
- **Regression gate:** `npm run verify:imessage` (`scripts/verify-imessage.ts`) drives the REAL route handlers against in-memory fakes (via `require.cache` pre-population — tsx compiles this repo to CommonJS, so ESM loader hooks never see the routes' internal imports) — no DB, no network — and exits non-zero on failure. 132 checks, and the harness itself is proven load-bearing: deliberately making `buildEventPreview` emit `subject_user_id`/`description` makes it exit 1 with 8 failures naming exactly those keys (mutation reverted; file byte-identical afterwards). It pins the authorization rules AND the preview's privacy contract by seeding sentinel usernames/notes/descriptions and asserting they never appear in the serialized response. **Extend it whenever you add a field to the preview payload**; it is the only thing standing between a careless `...event` spread and leaking a quiet bet's subject.
- **By:** n6 · 2026-08-16

## n6 integration: n6's iMessage routes migrated off the push API n4 deleted
- **Did:** Rebased n6's Messages-extension commit (`wymyzuxvoulq`) onto `redesign/midnight-glass` — it had been built on a pre-n4 base and was NOT an ancestor of the bookmark, so its 26-path change set was invisible in the real checkout. The rebase itself conflicted only in `DECISIONS.md` (6-sided) and `package.json` (4-sided), both resolved by the recorded UNION rule and verified mechanically (0 content lines lost, 0 invented, both files). But the rebase surfaced a REAL cross-node break that neither node could see alone: n6's two write routes imported `sendPushToAllSubscribers` from `lib/push.ts`, an export n4 **removed** when it replaced platform-wide broadcast with the audience-scoped filter. Root `tsc` failed with TS2305 on both routes, and n4's own gate (`verify:notifications` check 6, "no file under app/ imports sendPushToAllSubscribers") would have failed too.
- **Interfaces:** `app/api/imessage/bets/route.ts` and `app/api/imessage/bets/side/route.ts` now call `notifyEventAudience({ eventId, category: 'bets', payload, excludeUserIds })` — the same call shape as `app/api/events/route.ts` and `app/api/bets/route.ts`. The create route also now calls `applySubjectPrivacy(newEvent.id, subjectUserId, notifySubject)` right after `db.events.create`, which it was missing entirely: it accepted and stored `subject_user_id`/`notify_subject` but never wrote the `subject_hidden` mute row, so a quiet bet created from iMessage would have notified its subject. `scripts/verify-imessage.ts` fakes `notifyEventAudience`/`applySubjectPrivacy` instead of the deleted export, and gained 7 checks asserting both routes notify the EVENT's audience (never a broadcast) and exclude the actor. 132 -> 139 checks.
- **Why it matters beyond the typecheck:** the old call notified every push subscriber on the platform about a bet in a private group. Going through `notifyEventAudience` means iMessage-created bets now honor group membership, per-user category preferences and subject privacy exactly like the web/mobile paths — one notification filter, no bypass.
- **Verified in the REAL checkout** (`jj workspace root` = `/Users/viraat/code/wagerpals`, not a worktree): `app/api/imessage/session/route.ts` and `lib/imessage-share.ts` present on disk; `npx tsx scripts/verify-imessage.ts` -> 139/139, exit 0; `npx tsx scripts/verify-notifications.ts` -> 14/14, exit 0; `npx tsc --noEmit` -> zero errors outside the gitignored stale `.next/` cache and the pre-existing missing `sharp` dep in `scripts/generate-brand-assets.ts`.
- **By:** n6-integration · 2026-08-16

## n7: mobile ships no new native modules — the date picker is pure JS
- **Decision.** The iPhone polish node adds **zero npm dependencies** to `mobile/package.json`. Audit finding D13 ("event creation asks users to hand-type a date string") is closed with a hand-built pure-JS `DateTimeField` in `mobile/src/components/`, not with `@react-native-community/datetimepicker`.
- **Rationale.** `mobile/ios/` is gitignored and regenerated by `expo prebuild` from `mobile/plugins/imessage-extension/withIMessageExtension.js` (n6). Adding a native module changes the pod graph, which means the only honest verification is a full prebuild + `xcodebuild`, and a half-applied native dep would break n6's Messages-extension build for everyone. A JS picker is fully verifiable with `npx tsc --noEmit` here and cannot regress the extension.
- **Consequence for later nodes.** If someone later does want the system picker, it must land together with a prebuild + `xcodebuild -scheme WagerPalsMessages` run, not on its own.
- **Owning node:** n7

## n7: iPhone app ship-quality pass — mobile-only, plus three findings other nodes must close
- **Did:** Rebuilt the whole `mobile/src` UI layer: a 20-file shared component kit (`ErrorBoundary`, `ScreenState`, `Skeleton`, `Card`, `Button`, `Pill`, `Avatar`, `Money`, `ListRow`, `ProgressBar`/`SplitBar`, `FormScreen`, `Field`, `DateTimeField`, `AmountInput`, `SegmentedControl`, `Toggle`, `UserPicker`, `BottomSheet`, `SectionHeader`, `TextInputModal`), a rewritten `services/api.ts`, two new screens (`WalletScreen`, `NotificationPreferencesScreen`), and every existing screen brought to loading/empty/error + pull-to-refresh + keyboard-safe + safe-area + 44pt + overflow-safe. `docs/MOBILE_QA.md` records the method and result for each check.
- **Audit §D status:** D1, D3, D4, D5, D6, D7, D8, D9, D12, D13 closed. **D2 is stale** — RN 0.81.4 *does* implement `URL.searchParams` (`URL.js:173` + `URLSearchParams.js:27` strips the leading `?`), verified in the installed `node_modules`; no change was needed or made. **D10 mitigated, not fixed** — the empty-id defect is in `auth.ts` (not n7's lane), so `RootNavigator` now refuses to treat an id-less user as signed in. **D11 client half only** — `createEvent` makes `creator_user_id`/`creator_username` required parameters so no mobile caller can skip the server's membership check.
- **Interfaces other nodes should know:** `apiService` gained `getHomeData`/`getEventScreenData`/`getGroupScreenData` (parallel, optional legs degrade to `null`), `getWallet`/`createDeposit`/`withdraw`, `getNotificationPreferences`/`updateNotificationPreferences`, `getBets`/`deleteBet`, plus `invalidate(prefix)`, `invalidateForRefresh(...prefixes)`, `clearCache()` and `prefetch()`. Every failure is now an `ApiError` (`mobile/src/utils/errors.ts`) carrying `.kind`/`.status`/`.code`/`.userMessage` — render `.userMessage`, never `.message`. `getActivity` now REQUIRES a userId. `resolveEvent`/`unresolveEvent`/`deleteEvent` send `event_id`. New routes `Wallet` and `NotificationPreferences` in `RootStackParamList`.
- **D9 is fixed by a request header, not a client refresh endpoint.** Every request now sends `x-stack-refresh-token`; `lib/auth.ts` already reads it and lets Stack Auth mint a fresh access token server-side. There is deliberately no client-callable refresh route and none should be added. Note the client never receives a rotated access token, so each request pays that refresh — revisit if Stack Auth is configured to invalidate refresh tokens on use.
- **Two defects only visible at the seams between parallel workers**, both found in review and fixed: (1) the new SWR cache silently defeated pull-to-refresh — `request()` returns a cached entry whether fresh *or* stale, so a deliberate pull inside the TTL replayed the same bytes while the spinner implied a fetch; fixed centrally with `invalidateForRefresh`, called from all nine refresh handlers. (2) `GroupDetailScreen` still ejected to Safari for deposits against a **hardcoded production domain**, so in development it opened the live site.
- **Pre-existing bug fixed in passing:** `mobile/src/screens/index.ts` exported `GroupAdminScreen` under BOTH the `CreateEventScreen` and `EventDetailScreen` names (stale placeholders). Nothing imported the barrel, so it never shipped visibly — but any future `import { EventDetailScreen } from '../screens'` would have silently rendered the wrong screen.
- **Verified:** `cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit` 0 · root `npx tsc --noEmit` 0 · `npx tsx scripts/verify-imessage.ts` **139/139** · `npx tsx scripts/verify-notifications.ts` **14/14** · `npm run build` exit 0 (needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_STACK_PROJECT_ID`; supplied inline as a placeholder for the run, no `.env.local` written — same pre-existing constraint n4 recorded). `grep` for `#hex`/`rgba(` across `mobile/src/` matches **only `theme.ts`**. **NOT verified: nothing was run on a simulator or device, and the DB is unreachable — no deposit, withdrawal, resolve, payout or push was ever executed.** `npm run lint` cannot run: the repo has no ESLint config, so `next lint` prompts interactively.
- **FOR OTHER NODES — three things n7 could not close:**
  - **`app/api/events/route.ts` computes `resolver` only when `!group.is_public`**, but `events/resolve/route.ts` requires the resolver whenever `payment_type === 'cash' || !group.is_public`. For a **public group's cash event** the client is never told who may resolve it, so mobile shows the control optimistically and leans on the 403. Populate `resolver` whenever `payment_type === 'cash'` too.
  - **D11 server half** — enforce group membership unconditionally from the session, not `if (creator_user_id)`.
  - **D10 root cause** — `mobile/src/services/auth.ts:64` should throw rather than default a user id to `''`.
- **By:** n7 · 2026-08-16

- **`redesign/midnight-glass` is conflict-free again** (`e99347cf`), git ref exported and identical. Moving `@` forward re-surfaced the old `DECISIONS.md` conflict as a 2-sided one; it was a pure append-vs-append (n4's two sections on one side, n6's two completion reports on the other, empty base) and was resolved as a UNION per the recorded rule — n4's sections first, then n6's — and squashed into the bookmark commit so the bookmark itself is clean, not just the working copy.
- **n6's iMessage extension was deliberately NOT rebased onto the tip.** The objective named commit `e073e100de1f`, but that commit is **hidden/obsolete** — an older version of change `wymyzuxvoulq` whose `scripts/verify-imessage.ts` is 38 lines against the live version's 460. More importantly the n6 agent is **still running**: a top-level session plus seven Sonnet subagents, and its working-copy commit was rewritten five times during a two-minute inspection (`1b6a9a71 -> f0193e76 -> f7475837 -> 60c04f56 -> 8c017f3b -> ...`). Rebasing a live workspace's working-copy commit would have swapped files out from under those subagents mid-run and landed a half-verified snapshot on the mainline. A first rebase attempt against `1b6a9a71` proved the point — it hit an already-superseded commit and created a divergent `wymyzuxv/0` with a 7-sided `DECISIONS.md` conflict; that commit was abandoned and n6's own workspace was left byte-for-byte untouched.
- **n6 was then landed by another actor while this node waited, and it is now verified.** `redesign/midnight-glass` moved to `wymyzuxv 43a5c2b7`, a direct child of the conflict-free `syprpwmm e99347cf` above, with the base repo's `@` as its child — so n6 is now an ancestor of both `@` and the bookmark, which is what the objective asked for. Confirmed on disk in the real checkout: `lib/imessage-share.ts` (162), `docs/IMESSAGE.md` (445), `scripts/verify-imessage.ts` (1173), all four `app/api/{events/preview,imessage/*}` routes, `mobile/src/services/shared-session.ts`, all nine `mobile/plugins/imessage-extension/swift/*.swift`, `verify:imessage` registered, `package.json` parses. **`npx tsx scripts/verify-imessage.ts` -> 139/139** and **`verify-notifications.ts` -> 14/14** both pass from the same tree, so n4 and n6 coexist without regression; root `tsc` is clean apart from the pre-existing `sharp` gap, mobile `tsc` is 0.
- **CAUTION — the bookmark currently points at a LIVE working-copy commit.** `wymyzuxv 43a5c2b7` is workspace `rudder-17868792706127-c495ca`'s working copy and the n6 agent was still running (nine processes) at the time of writing. Every snapshot it takes rewrites that commit, moves the bookmark with it, and auto-rebases the base repo's `@` on top — so the base checkout's files track n6 live. That is fine while n6 finishes, but the bookmark should be re-pointed at a settled commit once it exits, and anything measured against the bookmark before then is a moving target.
