## worker: Recovered n7's full iPhone ship-quality pass out of its
- **Did:** Recovered n7's full iPhone ship-quality pass out of its orphaned worktree (objective-n7-iphone-app-ship-quality-bug-6ce3013d) into this registered jj workspace, and re-verified it end to end. Cause of the orphaning, from the op log: op 48509a9db6c2 added workspace rudder-20260816122956-6ce301, op 6e63f2a1f188 created its working-copy commit (mmkwqzwk 0714c629), then op 4a35ce48cb63 FORGOT the workspace — the tree survived on disk with a .jj/repo pointer but no registered workspace, so it can never be merged normally. Same failure as n4 and n6. This was an EXACT APPLY, not a merge: n7's forgotten commit 0714c629 had the single parent 43a5c2b7180f, which is exactly redesign/midnight-glass, and this workspace's @ sat on that same parent with an empty diff, so the base trees were byte-identical. Proof after copying: diff -rq between the two worktrees (excluding node_modules/.next/.jj/*.tsbuildinfo/next-env.d.ts/RUDDER.md/.env*) reports ZERO differences. I deliberately waited for n7's process (pid 71899) to exit at 06:13 before copying — it was still writing RootNavigator.tsx, docs/MOBILE_QA.md and DECISIONS.md when this run started — so the recovered tree is n7's finished state, not a mid-flight snapshot. Landed the full 47-path set (23 modified + 24 added): the 20-file mobile/src/components kit, WalletScreen + NotificationPreferencesScreen, utils/errors.ts + utils/format.ts, docs/MOBILE_QA.md, every existing mobile screen, both barrels, services/api.ts, types/{index,navigation}.ts, utils/helpers.ts, both navigators, DECISIONS.md. Zero deletions, zero package.json change, nothing touched under app/, lib/, components/, public/ or scripts/. VERIFIED, with every gate also run BEFORE the copy to establish a baseline so the passes are attributable: mobile tsc exit 0 (zero output); root tsc exit 0; verify-imessage 139/139; verify-notifications 14/14; npm run build exit 0 with a full route manifest. CORRECTION to a previously recorded fact: the 'pre-existing sharp gap' is not a repo defect — sharp@^0.34.5 is a declared devDependency present in package-lock.json that the base checkout's node_modules simply never had installed; installing it (node_modules only, no package.json/lockfile change) takes root tsc and npm run build to exit 0. Anyone re-running these gates in a fresh worktree must npm install first. I re-checked n7's own claims instead of trusting its QA doc; all hold (GroupDetailScreen's hardcoded wagerpals.io deposit eject gone, screens/index.ts barrel no longer aliases two screens to GroupAdminScreen, getActivity requires userId, resolve/unresolve/delete send event_id, ErrorBoundary mounted outside NavigationContainer, id-less auth user nulled before setUser, createEvent's creator params required, no hex/rgba outside theme.ts). ONE claim corrected and it is NOT a bug: n7 says invalidateForRefresh is called from 'all nine refresh handlers'; it is eight. The ninth screen with a RefreshControl (NotificationPreferencesScreen) correctly omits it because the SWR cache is opt-in and getNotificationPreferences does not opt in, so its pull-to-refresh genuinely hits the network. Separately CONFIRMED n7's cross-lane finding by reading both sides: app/api/events/route.ts:46 computes resolver only when group && !group.is_public, while app/api/events/resolve/route.ts:36 requires one whenever payment_type === 'cash' || (group && !group.is_public), so a public group's cash event hands the client resolver: null.
- **Interfaces:** No new interfaces authored by me — this recovery carries n7's surface through byte-identically. What later nodes must know: apiService (mobile/src/services/api.ts) gained getHomeData/getEventScreenData/getGroupScreenData (parallel, optional legs degrade to null), getWallet/createDeposit/withdraw, getNotificationPreferences/updateNotificationPreferences, getBets/deleteBet, plus invalidate(prefix), invalidateForRefresh(...prefixes), clearCache() and prefetch(fn). Every failure is an ApiError from mobile/src/utils/errors.ts carrying .kind/.status/.code/.userMessage — render .userMessage, never .message. The SWR cache is OPT-IN: request() caches a GET only when the caller passes { swr: { ttlMs } }; any cached GET behind a pull-to-refresh MUST call invalidateForRefresh first. getActivity(userId, opts) now REQUIRES a userId and throws client-side without one. resolveEvent/unresolveEvent/deleteEvent send event_id (not id). createEvent takes creator_user_id and creator_username as REQUIRED params. New Wallet and NotificationPreferences routes in RootStackParamList (mobile/src/types/navigation.ts); invite nav params are now optional. New shared kit under mobile/src/components/: ErrorBoundary, ScreenState, Skeleton, Card, Button, Pill, Avatar, Money, ListRow, ProgressBar, FormScreen, Field, DateTimeField, AmountInput, SegmentedControl, Toggle, UserPicker, BottomSheet, SectionHeader (plus the rewritten TextInputModal). DateTimeField is deliberately pure JS — mobile adds ZERO npm dependencies, so the n6 Messages-extension pod graph is untouched. Every request sends x-stack-refresh-token and Stack Auth mints a fresh access token server-side; there is deliberately no client-callable refresh endpoint and none should be added. Assumed present and unchanged: redesign/midnight-glass at 43a5c2b7 with n4's lib/push.ts (notifyUsers/notifyEventAudience) and n6's iMessage surface.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - Rudder must integrate this workspace to advance redesign/midnight-glass [out of lane] — The worker contract forbids me from running jj bookmark/commit/merge. The working copy holds the finished, verified recovery on parent 43a5c2b7, so integrating it advances the bookmark to exactly this tree. Nothing else is outstanding.
  - Delete the orphan worktree objective-n7-iphone-app-ship-quality-bug-6ce3013d [out of lane] — Its entire change set is recovered and verified here. It still has a .jj pointing at the shared repo with no registered workspace, so it will keep resurfacing as an unmergeable stray and inviting another hand recovery.
  - Fix the workspace-forgetting at the Rudder level — this is the third orphaning (n4, n6, n7) [out of lane] — Three in a row is systemic, not bad luck. Each one costs a full hand recovery run and risks a worker's output being lost if nobody notices before the tree is cleaned up. Worth finding why jj workspace forget fires against live workers instead of recovering a fourth time.
  - app/api/events/route.ts should populate resolver whenever payment_type === 'cash', not only when the group is private [out of lane] — Confirmed by reading both handlers: route.ts:46 gates on group && !group.is_public, resolve/route.ts:36 requires a resolver on payment_type === 'cash' || (group && !group.is_public). For a public group's cash event the client is handed resolver: null and can only discover the real resolver by eating a 403.
  - D11 server half: enforce group membership unconditionally from the session in app/api/events/route.ts [out of lane] — The route currently checks membership only inside if (creator_user_id). n7 closed the client half by making creator_user_id/creator_username required params so no mobile caller can omit them, but any non-mobile caller can still skip the check.
  - D10 root cause: mobile/src/services/auth.ts:64 should throw rather than default a user id to '' [out of lane] — n7 only mitigated it — RootNavigator now refuses to treat an id-less user as signed in, turning a logged-in app where every request fails into a recoverable auth screen. The defect that produces the empty id is still there.
  - Nothing in n7's pass ran on a simulator, a device, or a live database [out of lane] — No deposit, withdrawal, resolve, payout or push was ever executed; there is no iOS simulator and no reachable DB in this environment. Every result is tsc-, grep- or read-proven. A TestFlight pass is still required before calling the iPhone app ship-quality.
  - npm run lint cannot run — the repo has no ESLint config [out of lane] — next lint drops into an interactive 'How would you like to configure ESLint?' prompt, so it cannot run non-interactively in CI or in a worker. Adding a config touches root project files owned by other nodes.
- **By:** worker · 2026-08-16T13:18:25.471Z

## worker: Recovered all 4 orphaned followup fixes into this registered
- **Did:** Recovered all 4 orphaned followup fixes into this registered workspace (parent 24d1281e = redesign/midnight-glass) and re-ran each one's own verification from scratch. followup-3: scripts/test-sync-user.ts (890 lines) + its lib/sync-user.ts auth-method-merge and create-race fixes and the lib/db.ts username_selected `?? false` fix. followup-4: scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts + scripts/testing/stack-auth-stub.ts + README section. followup-5: constraintStatus() in scripts/migrate-comeback.ts now filters pg_constraint by owning relation + schema, both call sites updated. followup-6: escrow chips describe every player's stake via new escrow_by_bet on GET /api/events?id=, db.escrowHolds.getStatusByBetForEvent, and Ledger/page wiring, plus scripts/verify-escrow-chips.ts. Unlike n4/n6/n7 these four DID have registered jj workspaces, but all were (divergent) (conflict) on a stale parent line, so the work was on disk yet unmergeable and absent from the base. Extracted per-file `jj diff --git` patches instead of copying trees (their bases differ from midnight-glass, a tree copy would have reverted sibling work); all hunks applied clean except app/api/events/route.ts's import line, re-done by hand because this base carries n4's notifyEventAudience/applySubjectPrivacy where followup-6's base had sendPushToAllSubscribers. Dropped the DECISIONS.md and package.json hunks from every extraction (3-to-5-sided conflict blobs) and unioned the script entries onto the current package.json by hand. followup-5's verification only ever existed as throwaway DB probes it never saved, so it was rebuilt as scripts/verify-constraint-status.ts. VERIFIED: test:sync-user 30/30, test:auth 34/34, verify:escrow-chips 39/39, verify:constraints 20/20, db:verify 65/65, db:migrate 0 applied/44 present, npx tsc --noEmit clean except a pre-existing `sharp` error, npm run build 'Compiled successfully' failing only on that same sharp error. The sharp error is an incomplete node_modules install, not a defect — sharp@^0.34.5 is a declared devDependency; re-run gates after npm install.
- **Interfaces:** NEW db.escrowHolds.getStatusByBetForEvent(eventId) -> Promise<Record<betId, EscrowHoldStatus>> (lib/db.ts). GET /api/events?id= response gains escrow_by_bet: Record<betId, EscrowHoldStatus>, empty object for non-cash events. <Ledger> gains two optional props: eventStatus?: string and escrowByBet?: Record<string, EscrowHoldStatus>. BREAKING for new call sites: constraintStatus(table, constraintName) in scripts/migrate-comeback.ts now takes the owning table first. BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: db.users.create no longer coerces username_selected to true, it writes what you pass and defaults to false (syncUser is its only caller and its compensating UPDATE was removed). lib/sync-user.ts adds private uniqueViolationTarget(); no exported signature changed. New npm scripts: test:sync-user, test:auth, verify:escrow-chips, verify:constraints. New files: scripts/test-sync-user.ts, scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts, scripts/testing/stack-auth-stub.ts, scripts/verify-escrow-chips.ts, scripts/verify-constraint-status.ts.
- **Follow-ups:**

## n9: Comments are threaded, reactive and mention-aware — and `/api/comments` now authenticates every verb
- **Did:** Rebuilt the comment surface on the n1 schema (`parent_id`, `edited_at`, `deleted_at`, `comment_reactions`, `comment_mentions`). New `lib/comments.ts` holds the isomorphic rules shared by server and client (length cap 2000, depth cap 3, page size 20, reaction allowlist, mention parsing/segmentation, tree building, sliding-window rate limit, relative time). `app/api/comments/route.ts` grew PATCH and PUT, gained auth + membership checks on every verb including GET, and re-derives the author's username from `db.users.get(session.userId)` instead of trusting the request body. New `components/CommentThread.tsx` owns all comment fetching, optimistic writes and rollback; `components/CommentForm.tsx` is now a pure input component with @mention autocomplete and no network calls. Comments left the ledger and now live in their own `Comments (N)` section on the event page; event cards show `💬 N`.
- **Interfaces (other nodes must honour these):**
  - `GET /api/comments?eventId=X` **still returns a bare `Comment[]`** — the Expo client (`mobile/src/services/api.ts:572`) depends on that shape. All thread data is behind the opt-in `&view=thread`, which returns `{ comments, members, viewer, total_count, has_more, next_before }`. Do not change the default shape without changing mobile in the same commit.
  - Verbs: `POST` create/reply, `PATCH` edit (author only), `DELETE ?id=` tombstone (author **or** group admin), `PUT` toggle one emoji reaction. Reactions live on `PUT` of the same route rather than a sub-route, so the comments API stays one file.
  - **GET now requires auth** (public group **or** active member). This closes the audit finding that `/api/comments?eventId=` returned any event's comments to anyone. Reading a public group's thread is open to any signed-in user; **posting, editing and reacting always require active membership** — public visibility is not write access.
  - `EventWithStats.comment_count?: number` is populated **only** by `db.events.getAllWithStats` (a correlated subquery that excludes tombstones), not by the single-event GET. Treat it as optional everywhere.
- **Notification contract honoured, not worked around:** mentions dispatch through `notifyUsers({ category: 'mentions', eventId })` and everyone else through `notifyEventAudience({ category: 'comments', eventId })`, both excluding the author, and mentioned users are excluded from the broadcast so nobody is pinged twice. `eventId` is always passed, so n4's subject-privacy mute and per-user preferences filter inside the dispatcher. This route never touches a push transport, never reads a subscription, and never notifies a user the dispatcher would have suppressed.
- **Deletes tombstone, never cascade.** `comments.parent_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so a hard delete would silently take the replies with it. The route calls `db.comments.softDelete` only; `content` and `username` are scrubbed on read. A tombstone with no replies is dropped from the thread view entirely (nothing needs its context); one with replies renders as "Comment deleted" and keeps its children. **Do not add a hard-delete path to comments.**
- **Deep replies flatten, they do not error.** A reply past depth 3 is re-parented onto the deepest allowed ancestor by `resolveParentId`, so the UI can keep offering Reply at every level without producing an unreachable comment.
- **`lib/comments.ts` ships to the browser, so it is lookbehind-free.** A regex lookbehind is a *parse* error, not a failed match, on Safari < 16.4 — a module-level literal using one blanks the whole page rather than just breaking mentions. The mention regex consumes its boundary character instead. `npm run verify:comments` asserts this (and the absence of `server-only`/`@/lib/db`/`next/*`/`react` imports) so it cannot regress.
- **Rate limiting is per-process and deliberately so:** 5 posts / 20s and 20 / 5min in an in-memory sliding window. It is an abuse brake on a single burst, not a distributed limiter — anything authoritative across instances needs a shared store.
- **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npm run build` succeeds (34 routes); `npm run verify:comments` 10/10; live smoke test under `next start` — every write verb and both GET modes return 401 unauthenticated, a malformed body returns 400 not 500, and `GET /api/events` returns real `comment_count` values from the production database.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `npm run lint` **cannot run in this repo**: there is no ESLint config, so `next lint` drops into its interactive setup prompt and no-ops in CI. Someone should land a `.eslintrc.json` (`next/core-web-vitals`) as a standalone change — adding one inside a feature node would bury a repo-wide lint baseline in an unrelated diff [out of lane]
  - The thread view loads the whole event thread per request and pages in-process. Correct at current sizes; push the root selection into SQL (`LIMIT`/`OFFSET` over roots + a recursive descendant fetch) before threads reach the low thousands [n9]
  - Comments were removed from `components/Ledger.tsx`'s interleaved feed by no longer passing them in; Ledger's `comments`/`onCommentDeleted` props are now dead and can be deleted when someone owns that file [out of lane]
  - The Expo app still posts flat comments against the back-compat GET — threads, reactions and mentions are web-only until mobile adopts `&view=thread` [out of lane]
  - `generateId()` is still `Math.random()`-based for comment ids (audit finding); unchanged here, but a collision now also mis-parents replies [out of lane]
- **By:** n9 · 2026-08-16

## verify:payments is RED at redesign/midnight-glass (6127567b) because its test
- **What:** verify:payments is RED at redesign/midnight-glass (6127567b) because its test harness is stale, NOT because money handling is broken. The script authenticates by setting an 'x-stack-user-id' request header (scripts/verify-payments.ts:115). n2's identity consolidation deliberately made that header inert - it was a forged-identity vulnerability - and scripts/check-identity.ts Asserts 4-6 now assert it must NOT authenticate. Result: every authenticated call in verify-payments (POST /api/events, /api/bets, /api/wallet withdraw) returns 401, so Steps 3 onward all fail, while the DB-direct setup and the unauthenticated Stripe webhook steps still pass. Whoever fixes this must give the script a real Stack Auth session rather than re-adding the header backdoor - re-adding it would reopen the vulnerability and fail identity:check. Baseline at this commit: identity:check PASS, verify:notifications 14/14, verify:imessage 139/139, verify:comments 10/10, verify:payments FAIL (stale harness). Separately, 14 client call-sites under app/ still send the now-inert header; it is dead weight there because same-origin fetches carry the real session cookie.
- **By:** cli · 2026-08-16T14:01:58.555Z

## n8: The web surface has a flair layer — a tone system, a display face, and motion that means something
- **Did:** A whole-surface web design pass over all 14 routes and every component, in file-disjoint batches. Added an **additive "flair layer"** at the bottom of `app/globals.css` (n1's `:root` token block is untouched and remains the source of truth — the new layer only *composes* from it). Swapped the display face and gave every route a real focal point, AA-safe semantic colour, skeletons instead of spinners, empty states with a next action, and micro-interactions on the primary actions. 32 files changed, all inside `app/` (excluding `app/api/`) and `components/`.
- **Interfaces (other nodes must honour these):**
  - **The tone system replaces hardcoded semantic colour.** Put `tone-yes` | `tone-no` | `tone-pending` | `tone-info` | `tone-accent` | `tone-gold` | `tone-neutral` | `tone-win` | `tone-loss` on a wrapper; it sets `--tone-ink` / `--tone-solid` / `--tone-fill` / `--tone-line` and nothing else. Consume it with `.tone-text`, `.pill`, `.pill pill-solid`, `.tone-dot`, `.tone-surface`, `.rail`, `.rail-top`. Custom properties inherit, so setting the tone on a parent and consuming it on children is the intended pattern. **A `tone-*` class alone paints nothing** — it needs a consumer. Do NOT go back to `bg-green-50 border-green-200 text-green-700` string triples; that pattern is now gone from `app/` and `components/`.
  - **Every `--tone-ink` passes WCAG AA on white AND on its own `--tone-fill`** (measured: yes 5.02/4.55, no 6.47/5.73, pending 5.02/4.58, info 5.93/5.30, accent 6.70/6.02, gold 7.09/6.46, neutral 4.97 on fill). `tone-neutral`'s ink is `#646b78`, deliberately NOT `--color-text-secondary` (#6b7280), which measures 4.48:1 on that tone's own fill — just under AA. If you re-tint a fill, re-check the ink.
  - **`.tone-text` is declared LAST in the components layer on purpose.** Equal specificity means source order decides, and `.display-*` / `.stat-value` / `.market-title` / `.empty-state-title` all set `color`. Moving `.tone-text` earlier silently breaks every `display-2 tone-text tone-loss` combination the pages use. A Tailwind `text-*` utility still wins over it, which is intended.
  - **Gold is reserved.** `tone-gold` means money that was actually WON (payouts, winnings rows, leaderboard #1). It is the coin from n5's mark and it is the only non-blue accent. Do not use it as a generic highlight.
  - **New shared vocabulary, all in `app/globals.css`:** type (`.eyebrow`, `.eyebrow-accent`, `.display-1/2/3`, `.market-title`, `.numeral`, `.stat-value`, `.lede`); surfaces (`.card`, `.card-interactive`, `.card-focal`, `.panel`, `.section-head`, `.rule`, `.hairline`); layout (`.page-shell`, `.page-shell-narrow` — every route now uses one, so gutters match everywhere); market chrome (`.odds-track` + `.odds-fill odds-fill-yes|no`, already width-transitioned); controls (`.input`, `.input-invalid`, `.field-label`, `.field-hint`, `.btn-success`, `.btn-danger`, `.btn-quiet-success`, `.btn-quiet-danger`, `.press`, `.on-accent`); states (`.empty-state` + `-icon`/`-title`/`-body`, `.skeleton-line`). **This closes the repeated follow-up from n4, n5 and n9** asking for a hoisted `.input` and shared success/danger pill buttons — use these instead of re-inlining the strings.
  - **Display font changed: Space Grotesk → Bricolage Grotesque** in `app/layout.tsx`. The `--font-display` variable name and role are unchanged, so `font-display` consumers recompile untouched. **This includes n5's `Logo` lockup wordmark**, which deliberately renders in the ambient display face rather than hardcoding one — the "WagerPals" wordmark now reads wider and more characterful. `components/Logo.tsx`, the SVG mark, and every generated icon are untouched.
  - **A global `:focus-visible` ring now exists** (`:where(a,button,input,select,textarea,summary,[tabindex]):focus-visible`) plus a `.skip-link` as the first tab stop and `id="main"` on `<main>`. Do not add `outline-none` without a replacement — you no longer need to hand-roll focus styles.
  - **`prefers-reduced-motion` is handled once, globally.** Do not add per-component media queries. The block now also hard-disables the infinite animations (`.animate-urgent`, `.animate-pulse-glow`, `.animate-floaty`, `.animate-gradient`, `.live-dot::after`), which the previous duration-only override left running.
- **Behaviour is unchanged by design.** No data fetching, API call, request/response shape, auth header, route, validation rule or business logic was touched; `lib/`, `app/api/`, `tailwind.config.ts` and `components/Logo.tsx` have zero diff. New state is presentational only (odds-animation "previous value", copied/landed flags, focus management). Two additive OPTIONAL props exist so existing call sites compile unchanged: `Countdown` gained `variant?: 'inline' | 'pill'`, `CommentForm` gained `replyingToUsername?` and `textareaId?`.
- **One dead-code removal:** `components/Ledger.tsx` no longer renders comments or their delete handler. This was already unreachable — n9 stopped passing `comments`/`onCommentDeleted`, and `app/events/[id]/page.tsx` is Ledger's only call site — so this closes n9's "Ledger's comment props are dead" follow-up. The props remain on `LedgerProps` for call-site compatibility.
- **Verified:** root `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; clean `npm run build` exit 0 (44 route entries = 43 route files + Next's synthesized `/_not-found`; no route lost); `npm run verify:comments` 10/10. All 15 web routes serve HTTP 200 under `next start`, including `/events/[id]`, `/groups/[id]`, `/groups/[id]/admin` and `/groups/join/[id]` with unknown ids. Compiled CSS asserted to contain every new class, the `:focus-visible` rule, the reduced-motion block, and the Bricolage font-face; `.tone-text` confirmed to be emitted after `.stat-value`. Contrast ratios computed numerically (above), not eyeballed. `npm run lint` still CANNOT run — this repo has no ESLint config and `next lint` drops into an interactive prompt; that is n9's open follow-up, not a regression here.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - No browser-based visual regression or real-device check was possible in this environment [out of lane] — responsive behaviour at 375/768/1440 was built to spec and audited statically (no fixed width over 140px, every grid has a mobile base, long strings truncate), but nobody has looked at it in a real viewport.
  - The mobile bottom nav is still 6 tabs at `grid-cols-6` [out of lane] — ~62px per tab at 375px. It fits and labels do not clip, but n0 flagged this long ago and it stays tight; consolidating tabs is a product decision.
  - 12 routes still print Next's "deopted into client-side rendering" warning [out of lane] — caused by Stack Auth's `suspendIfSsr()` in `useUser`, pre-existing and untouched here (auth-hook usage is byte-identical to the base commit). It means those pages ship an empty SSR shell, which undercuts the skeletons; worth fixing at the auth-integration layer.
  - `app/invite/page.tsx` is a server component by design (it produces per-invite OpenGraph metadata for iMessage unfurls), so its "Copy link" is a `select-all` field rather than a JS copy button with a confirm micro-interaction [n8, accepted] — adding `'use client'` would break the metadata export.
  - `.text-gradient` / `.text-gradient-cool` / `.glass*` / `.chip*` are now unused by `app/` and `components/` [out of lane] — left defined in `globals.css` since they are n1's, and mobile/docs may reference the names. A later cleanup can drop them.

## 1786890775091905000-fix-lib-sync-user-ts-syncexistinguser-called-fro-20557: syncExistingUser in lib/sync-user.ts now checks existing.merged_into before pat…
- **Did:** syncExistingUser in lib/sync-user.ts now checks existing.merged_into before patching. When set, resolveCanonicalUser() walks the merged_into chain via db.users.get (multi-hop; refuses cycles, refuses a pointer at a missing row) and the sync targets the SURVIVING account: its auth_methods gain the sign-in method, last_seen_at/display_name/avatar are refreshed on it, and syncUser returns that row — the tombstone is never UPDATEd. Email policy on the redirect path: the survivor keeps its own address (a retired account must not rename the account that absorbed it); an email-less survivor adopts the verified address only when no other row (incl. the tombstone) still holds it, so the write cannot trip idx_users_email_lower. resolveExplicitUsername is now checked against the survivor id, so re-confirming the survivor own username is not a false conflict. An unresolvable chain fails closed (500) instead of resurrecting the tombstone.
- **Interfaces:** lib/sync-user.ts: new module-private MAX_MERGE_HOPS=16, type CanonicalResolution, async resolveCanonicalUser(tombstone: User). syncExistingUser signature unchanged but now writes to a resolved target row. CONTRACT CHANGE for callers: syncUser() may return a User whose id is NOT stackUser.id — take the acting id from syncResult.user.id. app/api/users/route.ts (returns result.user) and app/api/imessage/{bets,bets/side,session}/route.ts already do this; verified, no changes needed. scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts: new section 11 (6 checks) using the real route handlers + real Postgres. scripts/test-sync-user.ts: the old pinned test asserting the resurrect behaviour was deliberately replaced by 10 redirect tests. Recorded in DECISIONS.md.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - GET /api/users?id=<survivor> returns the public shape to a merged-away session [out of lane] — lib/auth resolves the session to the tombstoned Stack id, so route.ts compares authUserId === id and strips email/auth_methods when a redirected client re-reads its own (canonical) row. The POST response already carries the full user so nothing is broken today, but a client that re-fetches by id loses its identity fields.
  - Verify this is merged on disk in the BASE checkout before n10 starts [out of lane] — Task brief explicitly gates n10 on this landing in /Users/viraat/code/wagerpals (not a worktree). Several sibling nodes in this fleet were lost to orphaned workspaces, so confirm lib/sync-user.ts contains resolveCanonicalUser in the base checkout before unblocking n10.
- **By:** 1786890775091905000-fix-lib-sync-user-ts-syncexistinguser-called-fro-20557 · 2026-08-16T14:43:13.393Z

## RECOVERING AN ORPHANED WORKER WORKTREE: never copy its files
- **What:** RECOVERING AN ORPHANED WORKER WORKTREE: never copy its files onto the bookmark tip wholesale. Rudder has now lost the working-copy commit of five consecutive worker workspaces (n4, n6, n7, n9, n8), each leaving an EMPTY change with the real edits stranded on disk, so hand recovery is routine. The trap: a worker branches from BASE, other commits land on the bookmark while it runs, and copying the worker's version of a shared file silently REVERTS those commits' changes to it. This actually happened recovering n8 - it reverted 52a0fb88's escrow-chips fix in components/Ledger.tsx and app/events/[id]/page.tsx, and nothing caught it: tsc passed, the build passed, the route set was unchanged, the feature was just gone. Correct procedure: for every file the worker changed, check whether the bookmark also changed it since BASE; if so it is an OVERLAP and needs a 3-way merge (ancestor=BASE, ours=worker, theirs=tip) rather than a copy. Always exclude RUDDER.md (orchestrator-owned), .env.local and RUDDER_SHARED.md (secrets), and gitignored artifacts (tsconfig.tsbuildinfo, next-env.d.ts); resolve DECISIONS.md by union, never overwrite. SEPARATE JJ TRAP that makes this worse: jj treats [ and ] as fileset syntax, so 'jj file show -r REV app/events/[id]/page.tsx' returns EMPTY rather than erroring for every Next.js dynamic route. Unquoted, it reads as 'this file is empty at that commit' and invites you to restore it from nothing. Always quote as file:"<path>". A validated recovery script implementing all of this - including a regression test against the n8 case - is worth adding to scripts/ if the orphaning continues.
- **By:** cli · 2026-08-16T14:55:46.205Z

Recovered and landed from its orphaned worktree (sixth orphaning: n4, n6, n7, n9, n8, n10) - all verified in the base repo: root+mobile tsc clean, npm run build exit 0 (43 routes, unchanged), verify:payments 270/270 (was fully RED), verify:comments 10/10, verify:notifications 14/14, verify:imessage 139/139, verify:escrow-chips 39/39, identity:check PASS, test:sync-user 30/30, and npm run lint now RUNS (exit 0, 13 warnings, 0 errors). WHAT LANDED: the payments harness rewritten to use a real Stack Auth session via scripts/testing/stack-auth-stub.ts - the x-stack-user-id backdoor was NOT re-added (the two remaining mentions are comments explaining why it is not used, and identity:check still passes); A11 open-redirect fixed via a new lib/auth-redirect.ts exact-origin allowlist used by both mobile-oauth-callback and mobile-session; B2 withdrawals; an ESLint baseline; CLAUDE.md; a rewritten README.md; and docs/.audit-status-AB.md + docs/.audit-status-CDE.md holding the per-finding closure record. TWO THINGS FUTURE WORK MUST NOT MISS. (1) B2 is a DELIBERATE USER-VISIBLE CAPABILITY REMOVAL: withdrawals are now gated behind WALLET_WITHDRAWALS_ENABLED !== 'true' returning 503, checked BEFORE withdrawFromWallet is called so a disabled deployment cannot debit, and the transaction records status 'pending' instead of a false 'completed'. Rationale: the old path debited the wallet and never paid out. Re-enabling requires real Stripe Connect payouts, which n10 was told not to build. (2) E1 IS STILL A LIVE CRITICAL and did NOT get fixed - the fix was dispatched but lost to the termination. GET /api/groups?id= and GET /api/groups/members?groupId= have NO requireAuth and NO membership check; group IDs are 6 digits (~900k keyspace), so any group's name, full active member roster and pending join requests are enumerable by anyone. GET /api/users also still calls db.users.getAll() unauthenticated, exposing every user's net_total/total_bet/streak. docs/.audit-status-CDE.md documents this honestly as NOT FIXED. ALSO MISSING: docs/SMOKE_TEST.md was never written, and docs/AUDIT.md itself was never annotated with FIXED/DEFERRED marks (that record lives in the two .audit-status-* docs instead).

## sync-user: a merged-away Stack id now resolves to the surviving account (ON DISK, verified in the base checkout)
- **What:** `syncUser()` may now return a `User` whose `id` is NOT the signed-in Stack Auth id. When `db.users.get(stackUser.id)` finds a row with `merged_into` set (a tombstone left by `scripts/merge-duplicate-users.ts`), `syncExistingUser` follows the `merged_into` chain via `resolveCanonicalUser()` (multi-hop, `MAX_MERGE_HOPS=16`, refuses cycles, refuses a pointer at a missing row) and syncs/returns the SURVIVING account instead of patching the tombstone. An unresolvable chain fails closed with `{ok:false, status:500}` rather than handing the tombstone back.
- **Contract for callers:** take the acting user id from `syncResult.user.id`, never from the session/`stackUser.id`. Re-audited on this tip: `app/api/users` (returns `result.user`) and all three `app/api/imessage/*` routes already comply. Anything comparing a session id to a row id after a sync must use the returned id.
- **Email policy on the redirect path:** the survivor's own email always wins (a retired account never renames the account that absorbed it); a survivor with no email adopts the verified address only when no other row — including the tombstone — still holds it, so the write cannot trip `idx_users_email_lower`. `resolveExplicitUsername` is checked against the survivor's id, so re-confirming the survivor's own username is not a false conflict.
- **Why:** signing in on a merged-away Stack account used to patch the tombstone and return it, quietly resurrecting the duplicate identity the merge had just retired — the opposite of "one human, one account".
- **Provenance / why this entry exists:** the fix was authored in orphaned worktree `e9aca759` (commit `qlywmzup c547e759`) and then re-staged in workspace `4ebade59` (`qxxxlwxv f882cd6e`). BOTH were honest about being workspace-only, but Rudder integration never ran, so `lib/sync-user.ts` on disk at `/Users/viraat/code/wagerpals` still had ZERO `merged_into` references and `test:sync-user` was still running the pre-fix 30-check suite. Fixed by extracting `git diff 079a5dcfc63d c547e759c44c` for the three source files and writing them into the base checkout directly — safe because `685f60c6` (the tip) touches none of them, so the base copies were byte-identical to the fix's parent and no intervening commit could be reverted.
- **Verified IN THE BASE CHECKOUT (`/Users/viraat/code/wagerpals`, not a worktree):** `grep -c merged_into lib/sync-user.ts` → **9**; `npm run test:sync-user` → **39/39** (the suite grew 30→39; the old pinned test asserting the resurrect behaviour was deliberately replaced by 10 redirect tests, so any report citing "30/30" predates the fix); `npm run test:auth` → **40/40** against a real throwaway Postgres (section 11 covers redirect, tombstone left untouched, multi-hop chain, cycle-fails-closed); `npx tsc --noEmit` → exit 0; `npm run identity:check` → PASS.
- **Files:** `lib/sync-user.ts` (+94/-19), `scripts/test-sync-user.ts` (+142), `scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts` (+125).
- **By:** worker · 2026-08-16

## worker: Closed the group half of E1. GET /api/groups?id= and
- **Did:** Closed the group half of E1. GET /api/groups?id= and GET /api/groups/members?groupId= had no requireAuth and no membership check, so any 6-digit group id (~900k keyspace) yielded a group's name, full active roster (ids+usernames+roles) and pending join queue to an anonymous caller; ?userId= took the subject from the query string, letting any caller read any user's group list. Both now call requireAuth BEFORE the group lookup (so 404-vs-200 can't be used to enumerate group ids) and then branch on the caller's own membership row. Non-members of ?id= get an invite-preview only (name, is_public, created_at, member_count, admin_count, viewer_status) with members:[], pending_requests:[], resolver:null and no created_by/resolver_user_id -- an invite link IS the group id, so a flat 403 would break the join flow; /api/groups/members has no preview mode and 403s non-members. A 'pending' applicant counts as a non-member. ?userId= now goes through verifyUserMatch (403 on mismatch) and is optional -- omitted, the caller comes from the session. ?public=true is untouched and stays anonymous (counts only, never rosters). VERIFIED: new npm run verify:groups-auth (scripts/verify-groups-auth.ts, 77/77, no DB) drives both REAL route handlers against the REAL lib/auth.ts with only Stack Auth stubbed via the same module.registerHooks redirect test:auth uses; negative control -- reverting both routes to pre-fix source fails 40+ of those same checks including 'anonymous ?id=123456 -> 401 (observed 200)' with the roster in the body. Real unauthenticated curl against the shared Neon dev DB, real private group 948584 'Cabin Group' (5 active members): ?id= 401, ?userId=<real member uuid> 401, /api/groups/members?groupId= 401, bare /api/groups 401, forged x-stack-user-id header 401, ?public=true 200; with the pre-fix route hot-reloaded into the SAME running server the dev log records 'GET /api/groups?id=948584 200' for the identical anonymous request. npx tsc --noEmit clean, npm run build exit 0.
- **Interfaces:** NEW response fields on GET /api/groups?id=: viewer_status ('active'|'pending'|null -- the CALLER's own membership state), is_member (bool), is_admin (bool), user_role ('admin'|'member'|null). members / pending_requests are still ALWAYS arrays (empty for non-members) so existing .find/.some call sites don't crash; code deciding 'am I an admin here' should read is_admin rather than scanning the roster. GET /api/groups?userId= is now optional (derived from session when absent) and 403s on mismatch. GET /api/groups/members?groupId= returns 401 unauthenticated / 403 for non-members. NEW npm script verify:groups-auth -> scripts/verify-groups-auth.ts (no DB, no network). Call sites updated: app/groups/join/[id]/page.tsx reads viewer_status (array scan kept as fallback); app/groups/[id]/page.tsx redirects is_member===false to the join page; app/groups/[id]/admin/page.tsx made defensive. Mobile needed no change -- it only navigates into groups the signed-in user is an active member of and already sends the bearer token on GETs. CLAUDE.md gained a section 8 invariant ('Group membership is the read boundary') and a section 6 table row.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - GET /api/users with no params still enumerates every user unauthenticated [out of lane] — This is the remaining half of E1: app/api/users/route.ts:50 calls db.users.getAll() with no auth check and returns every user (redacted through toPublicUser, but a full enumeration nonetheless). Left alone deliberately -- that file is owned by the in-flight identity work and is gated by test:auth and identity:check, so editing it here would collide with a sibling.
  - Group ids are a 6-digit invite secret with no rate limiting [out of lane] — The invite preview on GET /api/groups?id= now requires a signed-in caller, but any signed-in caller can still walk the ~900k id space and harvest group names + member counts. If group names are considered sensitive, the invite needs its own unguessable token (like lib/imessage-share.ts already does for events) or the endpoint needs rate limiting.
  - db.groups.getByUser already filters to active memberships, making is_member always true on the ?userId= leg [out of lane] — The per-group is_member/user_role computation in the ?userId= branch re-derives what the SQL already guarantees. Harmless but dead-ish; worth collapsing when someone owns that file for cleanup.
- **By:** worker · 2026-08-16T15:56:08.252Z

## worker: E1 (part 1 of 2) — group rosters are now members-only on both group read endpoints
- **Did:** Closed the unauthenticated group-disclosure half of E1. `GET /api/groups?id=` and `GET /api/groups/members?groupId=` both had **no** `requireAuth` and **no** membership check; with a 6-digit group id (~900k keyspace) anyone could pull any group's name, full active roster (user ids + usernames + roles) and pending join queue. `GET /api/groups?userId=` was worse than unauthenticated — it took the subject's id straight from the query string, so any caller could read **any user's** group list by naming them.
- **The gate:** `requireAuth` runs before the group is looked up (so an anonymous caller can't use 404-vs-200 to enumerate group ids), then the caller's own membership row decides what comes back. `?userId=` now goes through `verifyUserMatch` (403 on mismatch) and is **optional** — omitted, the caller is derived from the session. `?public=true` is untouched and stays anonymous: it lists public groups with counts only, never rosters.
- **Non-members get an invite preview, not a 403.** An invite link IS the group id, so a 403 would break the join flow. `?id=` returns `name`, `is_public`, `created_at`, `member_count`, `admin_count` and `viewer_status` (the caller's OWN membership state: `null` / `'pending'`), with `members: []`, `pending_requests: []`, `resolver: null` and **no** `created_by` / `resolver_user_id`. `/api/groups/members` has no preview mode — a non-member gets 403. A `pending` applicant is treated as a non-member throughout.
- **Interfaces (new response fields on `GET /api/groups?id=`):** `viewer_status: 'active' | 'pending' | null`, `is_member: boolean`, `is_admin: boolean`, `user_role: 'admin' | 'member' | null`. `members` / `pending_requests` are still always arrays, so existing `.find`/`.some` call sites do not crash — they just come back empty for non-members. Anyone reading the roster to decide "am I an admin here" should read `is_admin` instead.
- **Call sites updated:** `app/groups/join/[id]/page.tsx` now reads `viewer_status` (falling back to the array scan) — it was the one legitimate non-member caller of `?id=`. `app/groups/[id]/page.tsx` redirects a non-member (`is_member === false`) to the join page instead of rendering an empty group. `app/groups/[id]/admin/page.tsx` made defensive. Mobile needed no change: it only ever navigates into groups the signed-in user is an active member of, and `apiService` already sends the bearer token on GETs.
- **Verified:** new `npm run verify:groups-auth` (`scripts/verify-groups-auth.ts`, **77/77**, no DB) drives both real route handlers against the **real** `lib/auth.ts` — only Stack Auth is stubbed, via the same `module.registerHooks` redirect `test:auth` uses — plus in-memory `lib/db`/`lib/push`. **Negative control run:** reverting both routes to their pre-fix source and re-running the same harness fails 40+ checks, including `anonymous ?id=123456 → 401 (observed 200)` with the full roster in the body, so the harness demonstrably detects the real bug. Also `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Real unauthenticated curl, real database.** Against a dev server on the shared Neon dev DB, group `948584` ("Cabin Group", private, 5 active members): `?id=948584` → **401**, `?userId=<real member uuid>` → **401**, `/api/groups/members?groupId=948584` → **401**, bare `/api/groups` → **401**, `?id=` with a forged `x-stack-user-id` header → **401**, and `?public=true` → **200**. On the SAME running server with the pre-fix route hot-reloaded back in, the dev log records `GET /api/groups?id=948584 200` for that identical anonymous request.
- **E1 IS NOT FULLY CLOSED.** `GET /api/users` with no params still calls `db.users.getAll()` unauthenticated and returns every user (redacted through `toPublicUser`, but still a full enumeration). Deliberately left alone: `app/api/users/route.ts` is owned by the in-flight identity work and is gated by `test:auth` / `identity:check`, so touching it here would collide. It needs its own task. [out of lane]
- **By:** worker (app/api/groups GET auth) · 2026-08-16

- **Did:** Carried the three named uncommitted files (lib/sync-user.ts, scripts/test-sync-user.ts, scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts) from the shared base checkout into this isolated workspace so Rudder commits them into a described commit, and verified them. The change makes syncUser() tombstone-aware: a sign-in on a row carrying merged_into now resolves through the merge chain to the surviving account instead of patching the retired duplicate. Adds resolveCanonicalUser() with MAX_MERGE_HOPS=16, cycle detection and dangling-pointer detection, all failing closed with a 500 rather than reviving a retired identity; email ownership is re-resolved against the survivor so a retired account can never rename the account that absorbed it, and an address the tombstone still holds is not copied across (would trip idx_users_email_lower and turn a sign-in into a 500). None are exported - syncUser()s public signature and SyncUserResult are unchanged, so no caller needs updating. Behavioral change visible to callers of POST /api/users: signing in on a merged-away Stack Auth id now returns the SURVIVING users row (different id than the authenticated Stack id) rather than the tombstone; an unresolvable chain (cycle, dangling merged_into, >16 hops) now returns 500 Failed to sync user where it previously silently patched the tombstone.

Not a failure and unrelated to the tombstone fix, but it weakens the one-human-one-row guarantee since the merge tool groups by candidate email.

## GET /api/users no-params directory now requires auth (audit finding E1, part 2)

- **Decision:** `app/api/users/route.ts` GET calls `requireAuth(request)` before `db.users.getAll()`, so the no-params branch — a bulk scrape of every user's username/display_name/avatar_url/net_total/streak — returns 401 to an anonymous caller. This closes the half of audit finding E1 that the groups-auth worker deliberately left out of its lane. Auth is checked BEFORE the DB read so an anonymous request cannot learn the user count or infer anything from response timing.
- **Deliberately NOT changed:** the `?id=` and `?username=` branches stay reachable without a session. They resolve one named row at a time through `toPublicUser()` (email, auth_methods, merged_into, last_seen_at stripped), and `?id=<self>` still returns the caller's full row when the session matches. Gating them was not in the ask and would need a sweep of the sign-in/join pages that call them.
- **Interfaces:** `GET /api/users` (no params) → 401 `{error:'Authentication required'}` when unauthenticated, 200 array of public users when signed in (web cookie or mobile bearer). `?id=` / `?username=` unchanged. New `npm run verify:users-auth` (no DB). New CLAUDE.md §8 invariant: "The user directory is never anonymous", and a §6 table row.
- **Verified:** `npm run verify:users-auth` 49/49 in BOTH the workspace and the real checkout; negative control confirmed (gate removed → 16 of the 49 fail, incl. "auth is checked BEFORE db.users.getAll()"). Real HTTP against `next dev` in the real checkout `/Users/viraat/code/wagerpals`: anonymous `GET /api/users` → 401, forged `x-stack-user-id` → 401, bogus bearer → 401, `?id=` → 200, `?username=` → 200, both missing → 404. Live pre/post control on a running server against the Neon dev DB: gate removed → HTTP 200 with all 45 real users; gate restored → 401. Also `npm run identity:check` (Part A + B pass) and `npm run verify:groups-auth` 77/77, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Integration note:** the change was ALSO copied into the real checkout by hand (route.ts, package.json, CLAUDE.md, scripts/verify-users-auth.ts), after byte-comparing every target against `git show HEAD:<file>` to confirm no sibling edit was clobbered. Same hand-recovery pattern the previous two workers had to use.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `?username=` is an unauthenticated per-name oracle [out of lane] — anonymous callers can still confirm whether a username exists and read its net_total/streak one guess at a time. Bulk enumeration is closed, single-name probing is not.
  - `mobile/src/services/api.ts` `getAllUsers()` is dead code [out of lane] — it is the only caller of the no-params branch anywhere in web or mobile, and nothing calls it. It would keep working (the mobile client sends a bearer token), but it should probably be deleted.

## followup-3/4/5/6 landed: 424b5dfb rebased onto redesign/midnight-glass and verified in the base checkout

- **`jj rebase -r 424b5dfb -d redesign/midnight-glass` produced exactly three conflicts and all three were pure appends.** `package.json` (n9's `verify:comments` vs this commit's `verify:escrow-chips`/`verify:constraints` — all four scripts kept), `DECISIONS.md` (n9's section vs this commit's recovery section — both kept, n9 first), and `app/events/[id]/page.tsx`. Resolved as UNION per the recorded rule. Result `52a0fb88` is conflict-free and its diff against the old bookmark is byte-identical in shape to the original commit: the same 14 paths, 2587 insertions, 37 deletions.
- **The `page.tsx` conflict was NOT a mechanical union and is the one judgement call here.** n9 *removed* `Comment` from the `@/lib/types` import (comments moved out of `Ledger` into `CommentThread`); 424b5dfb *added* `EscrowHoldStatus` to the same line. A literal union would have re-imported `Comment`, which nothing in the file uses any more — an unused-import regression. The union taken is semantic: `import { EscrowHoldStatus, EventWithStats, NetResult }`. The `<Ledger>` call site merged cleanly and now carries n9's prop removal *and* followup-6's `eventStatus` / `escrowByBet`.
- **The objective's description of the followup-6 fix was wrong about the mechanism, not the outcome.** It expected `app/api/wallet/route.ts` to be loosened so `verifyUserMatch` stops blocking other players' escrow. That file is untouched, deliberately: followup-6 fixed the leak at a different seam by adding `db.escrowHolds.getStatusByBetForEvent()` and returning `escrow_by_bet` from `GET /api/events?id=`, keeping `/api/wallet` correctly scoped to the caller. Loosening the wallet route would have been the worse fix — it would expose one player's wallet to another to render a chip.

## n4 integration: `706926c26219` is now merged onto `redesign/midnight-glass` — no rebase was required
- **The premise in the objective was wrong in one important way, and that made the job trivial.** `706926c26219` is not an unrelated head that needed rebasing. It has the *exact same two parents* as `redesign/midnight-glass` (`utuxumkl f39c7d26` + `xyzvyztm ed70f573`) — both are independent resolutions of the same merge. So there was no drift to reconcile and no risk of clobbering n2/n3/n5: n4's base tree already contained them. Verified after the fact — `jj diff --from 706926c26219 --to @` shows **DECISIONS.md only**, i.e. every one of n4's 21 code files landed byte-identical, and `jj diff --from redesign/midnight-glass --to @` is **exactly n4's 23-path change set**.
- **What "conflict resolution" actually meant here.** Only the two files that were already conflicted needed a decision, and they resolved the same way the recorded UNION rule says:
  - **`package.json`** — the midnight-glass resolution and n4's resolution were character-identical except that n4 adds `"verify:notifications"`. Took n4's file wholesale; nothing from n2 (`identity:*`, `users:merge`, `stack:link-accounts`), n3 (`verify:payments`) or n5 (`brand:assets`) was dropped. Validated as parseable JSON.
  - **`DECISIONS.md`** — took the midnight-glass resolution (which already unions all five sides *and* carries six sections newer than n4's base) and appended n4's two sections verbatim after it. This is what the `n4-recovery` note above asked for: the sibling resolution wins, and n4's appends are re-applied on top of it rather than re-resolved a second time. **The 5-sided conflict is now settled — do not re-resolve it.**
- **`lib/push.ts` was never touched by n2 or n3**, contrary to the objective's guess. Confirmed structurally (it is identical to n4's version, and midnight-glass's copy is just the untouched base) and by reading the result: n4's rewritten `app/api/bets/route.ts` and `app/api/events/resolve/route.ts` still import and drive n3's `lib/payments` (`placeCashBet`, `settleCashEvent`, `MAX_TRANSACTION_AMOUNT`), and every push route authenticates through n2's `requireAuth` with zero `x-stack-user-id` call sites left.
- **Verified in this workspace from a clean `npm install`:** `npx tsc --noEmit` → **exit 0** · `npx tsx scripts/verify-notifications.ts` → **14/14 checks, exit 0** · `cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit` → **exit 0** · `npm run build` → **exit 0**, and its route manifest confirms the shape: `/api/push/preferences|subscribe|test` present, `send`/`cleanup`/`debug`/`verify-key`/`send-to-user` gone. The build needs a real `.env.local` (Stack Auth reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_STACK_PROJECT_ID` while collecting page data for `/api/auth/mobile-session`); it was copied in for that single run and deleted immediately after, so no credentials remain in the worktree. `tsc` and `verify:notifications` need neither env nor network.
- **Not run: `npm run verify:payments`.** It talks to the live Neon database and would mutate real rows. n4's change to it (swapping the `web-push`-only stub for a `setPushTransport()` stub so Expo sends are also intercepted) is carried through and type-checks, but it is unproven at runtime here.
- **The bookmark move is Rudder's to make, not mine.** Per this repo's worker contract I did not run `jj bookmark`/`commit`/`merge`; the working copy holds the finished merge result, so integrating it advances `redesign/midnight-glass` to exactly that tree.
- **n7 and n9 are unblocked** — the n4 surface they hard-depend on (`notifyUsers` / `notifyEventAudience` / `setPushTransport`, `GET|PATCH /api/push/preferences`, `mobile/src/services/notifications.ts`) is now on the mainline. n9 should wire `app/api/comments/route.ts` using the exact `notifyEventAudience` call spelled out in n4's section above.

## sync-user: a merged-away Stack id now resolves to the surviving account
- **What:** `syncUser()` may now return a `User` whose `id` is NOT the signed-in Stack Auth id. When `db.users.get(stackUser.id)` finds a row with `merged_into` set (a tombstone left by `scripts/merge-duplicate-users.ts`), `syncExistingUser` follows the `merged_into` chain (multi-hop, cycle- and dangling-pointer-safe) and syncs/returns the surviving account instead of patching the tombstone. An unresolvable chain (cycle, or a pointer at a row that no longer exists) fails closed with `{ok:false, status:500}` rather than handing the tombstone back.
- **Contract for callers:** take the acting user id from `syncResult.user.id`, never from the session/`stackUser.id`. `app/api/users` (returns `result.user`) and all three `app/api/imessage/*` routes already do. Anything comparing a session id to a row id after a sync must use the returned id.
- **Email policy on the redirect path:** the survivor's own email always wins (a retired account never renames the account that absorbed it); a survivor with no email adopts the verified address only when no other row — including the tombstone — still holds it, so the write cannot trip `idx_users_email_lower`. `resolveExplicitUsername` is now checked against the survivor's id, so re-confirming the survivor's own username is not a conflict.
- **Why:** signing in on a merged-away Stack account used to patch the tombstone and return it, quietly resurrecting the duplicate identity the merge had just retired — the opposite of "one human, one account".
- **Tests:** `scripts/test-sync-user.ts` (39 checks, no DB) — the old pinned test asserting the resurrect behaviour was deliberately replaced by 10 redirect tests. `scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts` section 11 (40 checks, real Postgres) covers redirect, tombstone left untouched, multi-hop chain, and cycle-fails-closed.
- **By:** worker · 2026-08-16
- **Recovered from:** `fix-lib-sync-user-ts-syncexistinguser-ca-e9aca759`

## n5-replacement: one glyph, one icon set — `scripts/brand/*.svg` now matches `components/Logo.tsx`, and `npm run brand:verify` keeps it that way
- **Did:** This node was spawned to build `components/Logo.tsx` and wire it into `Header.tsx`, but both had already landed from the integrated line by the time it started (Header renders `<Logo variant="lockup" animate="mount" markClassName=… />`). So the component was left alone — the real remaining defect was that the logo existed in **two** incompatible versions: `components/Logo.tsx` drew chevrons `M6,13 L18,52 L32,21` / `M32,21 L46,52 L58,13` with a coin at `(32,15) r=5.9`, while `scripts/brand/*.svg` — the artwork every icon is rasterized from — drew a completely different glyph (coin sitting *in* the apex at `(32,30) r=5`). The header and the installed app icon were not the same logo. Rewrote all three source marks to the component's exact path data, stroke width and coin geometry, then regenerated the whole asset set. Also fixed the second half of the failure: `public/manifest.json` referenced four icons of which only one existed, and `app/layout.tsx` referenced `/favicon.ico`, `/og.png` and `/icons/wagerpals-mark.svg` — none of which were in the repo. All 19 artifacts now exist and are served.
- **Interfaces:** New `scripts/verify-brand.ts` + `npm run brand:verify`. Read-only deploy gate, 26 checks: (a) the glyph contract — path data / stroke width / coin `cx,cy,r` compared literally between `components/Logo.tsx` and each `scripts/brand/*.svg`, plus `mark-silhouette.svg` must stay flat white for Android's alpha-only notification icon; (b) every asset path referenced by `manifest.json`, `app/layout.tsx`, `service-worker.js`, `Header.tsx`, `app/page.tsx` and `mobile/app.json` exists, is non-empty, and its real pixel size matches the size its referrer advertises; (c) `mobile/assets/icon.png` has no alpha (App Store) and the maskable icons' corners are background, not mark; (d) `Header.tsx` imports and renders `<Logo>` and does not re-fork the mark as a raw `<img>`. **If you change the artwork in either place you must change it in both and re-run `npm run brand:assets`** — the gate is what stops the header and the app icon drifting apart again.
- **Also:** `scripts/brand/*.svg` carry a `translate(0,-0.5)` wrapper (Logo.tsx uses `-1.5`). That is deliberate and commented in the files: the generator centres artwork by viewBox, and `-0.5` is what actually puts the ink's centre on `(32,32)`; the difference does not affect the glyph and the gate ignores transforms.
- **Verified:** `npm run brand:assets` regenerates 19 artifacts, self-check passes. `npm run brand:verify` = 26 checks / 0 problems, and was fault-injected four ways (nudged a path coordinate, coloured the silhouette, zeroed an icon, pointed the manifest at a missing file) — each was caught and the tree restored. Root `npx tsc --noEmit` 0 errors. `npm run build` succeeds. Under `next start`, all 12 icon/OG routes return 200 and `/icons/*`, `/favicon.ico`, `/og.png` are byte-identical to the generated files. `Header.tsx`'s exact `<Logo>` props were rendered through `react-dom/server` and rasterized: the mark draws correctly and carries the mount keyframes, the reduced-motion escape hatch and its `<title>`.
- **Resolved two inherited conflicts** so the tree builds: `package.json` (3-sided — union of the n1/n2/n3/n5 script entries; `db:verify` survived, closing followup-5's flag that package.json was invalid JSON in every workspace) and `DECISIONS.md` (union of the n2 identity entry with the followup/followup-5/audit entries).
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `npm run brand:verify` is not wired into any build or CI step [out of lane] — it only runs when someone types it. Adding it to a `prebuild` or CI job is what turns it from a script into an actual gate.
  - The `<body>` is empty in server-rendered HTML [out of lane] — `StackProvider` makes the whole tree client-only, so the header (and every page) renders nothing until hydration. Pre-existing and unrelated to the brand work, but it means no page has meaningful SSR content or first-paint markup.
  - `app/page.tsx:311` still points at `/icons/icon-192x192.svg` directly [out of lane] — correct artwork now, but it is a third place the mark is referenced by hand; it could use `<Logo variant="mark" />`.
- **By:** n5-replacement · 2026-08-16
- **Recovered from:** `n5-brand-identity-failed-3x-with-no-ship-c9d36eef`

## followup-2: the cash/escrow surface in `Ledger.tsx` — already landed, nothing to rebase
- **Finding: the code recovery was a no-op, and that is the result, not a shortcut.** followup-2 ("Complete Ledger.tsx modifications", workspace `complete-ledger-tsx-modifications-f4debca6`, change `qsnntwpk 3bdf9819`, divergent + conflicted) touched exactly three files: `components/Ledger.tsx`, `app/events/[id]/page.tsx`, `DECISIONS.md`. Diffing its **on-disk final state** against tip `52a0fb88` shows the only differences are followup-6's escrow-per-player work, which is a strict **superset** of followup-2's chip code. Every followup-2 hunk is already on tip. Rebasing it would have been at best a no-op and at worst a **regression**: a naive apply would have reinstated `HOLD_LABELS`/`holdByBetId` (viewer-scoped, `Map<string, EscrowHold>` off `walletSummary.event.holds` only) over followup-6's `HOLD_CHIPS`/`holdStatusByBetId` (event-wide, seeded from `escrowByBet`), reintroducing the exact bug `npm run verify:escrow-chips` exists to prevent. **Do not re-run a followup-2 recovery; there is nothing left in that worktree but this note.**
- **How it got there without a followup-2 merge:** the four-orphan recovery above extracted followup-6's `Ledger.tsx`, and followup-6's own base already carried followup-2's edits — so followup-2's work rode in as part of followup-6's file, never as its own integration. That is why `RUDDER.md` still lists followup-2 as `integration-blocked` while its code is live.
- **What followup-2 actually contributed** (all verified present in the real checkout, not a worktree — `/Users/viraat/code/wagerpals`): (1) an `eventStatus?: string` prop, passed as `event.status` from `app/events/[id]/page.tsx` and used as a refetch trigger — settling or cancelling an event moves money without changing the bet list, so the effect's bet-id-keyed deps never fired and escrow/pot went stale; (2) an explicit wallet-error card with a working "Try again" retry (`walletError` + `walletReloadKey` + `reloadWallet`), replacing a silent render-nothing on fetch failure in cash mode; (3) `WalletSummary.transactions` made optional to match what `getWalletSummary` really returns, plus `deposit`/`withdrawal` transaction labels; (4) per-bet Escrowed/Settled/Refunded chips — **since superseded** by followup-6, which widened them from the viewer's own bets to every player's and made the tooltip name whose stake it is; (5) a memoized `betSignature` replacing the `bets.length` + inline `bets.map().join()` dep pair, dropping the `eslint-disable react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`, and gating the skeleton on `walletLoading && !walletSummary` so it stops flashing over an already-loaded panel.
- **Verified on tip `52a0fb88`, with `node_modules` and `.env.local` APFS-cloned from the base checkout:** `npx tsc --noEmit` → **exit 0, zero errors** (the `sharp` error the four-orphan recovery reported is gone — it was the borrowed pre-n5 `node_modules`, confirming that diagnosis). `npm run verify:escrow-chips` → **39/39** against the live Neon dev DB, all probe rows cleaned up. Item-by-item presence check of contributions (1), (2), (3) and (5) against the **real checkout's** `components/Ledger.tsx` and `app/events/[id]/page.tsx` → 11 markers present, `eslint-disable` confirmed absent; contribution (4) is what the 39/39 run covers. Both files are **byte-identical** between the base checkout and this workspace, so the confirmation is of shipped state, not of a workspace copy.
- **Interfaces:** none added or changed. `<Ledger>`'s `eventStatus`/`escrowByBet` props and `GET /api/events?id=`'s `escrow_by_bet` were already recorded by the four-orphan recovery entry above; this change is documentation only.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - followup-2's original "escrow chips only appear on the viewer's own bets" follow-up is **DONE** — closed by followup-6 (`escrow_by_bet` on the event payload); it needs no further work and should not be re-spawned [in]
  - No verification covers followup-2's client-effect behaviours — the `eventStatus`-driven refetch, the error card and its retry, and the no-flash skeleton are all `useEffect`-driven, and `verify-escrow-chips.ts` uses `renderToStaticMarkup`, which never runs effects. Proving them needs a DOM environment (`jsdom`/`happy-dom`); deliberately not added here because it means a new devDependency plus `package.json`/`package-lock.json` churn, and those two files have been the repeated conflict source across this whole DAG [out of lane]
  - Delete button still renders for other users' non-cash bets — pre-existing; the API rejects it but the UI offers it to everyone. Worth hiding behind `bet.user_id === currentUserId` [out of lane]
  - Mobile `EventDetailScreen` has no wallet/escrow panel — `mobile/src/screens/EventDetailScreen.tsx` renders its own ledger and shows none of the cash/escrow state the web Ledger now shows [out of lane]

## worker: `verify:payments` now authenticates with a real Stack Auth session — 242/242, the payment engine is proven at runtime
- **Did:** Ported `scripts/verify-payments.ts` off the dead `x-stack-user-id` header and onto real Stack Auth sessions, using the exact mechanism `scripts/test-auth-consolidation.ts` already established: a `module.registerHooks` resolve hook points the one specifier `@/lib/stack` at `scripts/testing/stack-auth-stub.ts`, and nothing else is faked. `lib/auth.ts`, `requireAuth`, `verifyUserMatch` and every route's own authorization check are the shipped code. `callRoute`'s `userId` option now sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>` + `x-stack-refresh-token` for that human (the mobile path — per-request, so four humans can act in one process without a shared cookie deciding who they are), with an `accessToken` escape hatch for unknown-token checks. This closes the follow-up recorded at DECISIONS.md ~line 578: the 91 failures were all 401s and the money paths were unproven.
- **Result: `npm run verify:payments` → PASSED 242/242, exit 0**, against the live Neon dev database. Was 91 failing / 210. The 32 new checks are the auth gate; the other 210 are the original money assertions, now actually reaching the engine for the first time since n2.
- **Only `scripts/verify-payments.ts` changed.** No route, no `lib/`, no schema. The engine needed no fix — it was the harness that was broken. Every payment invariant (wallet balance === Σtransactions, event ledger sums to 0, no stranded `held` escrow, no negative balance) holds across deposits, fixed and open-stakes bets, resolve, unresolve, re-resolve, degenerate refund, cancel, free events and withdrawals.
- **New Step 0 is a permanent regression guard against the backdoor, not a comment.** It asserts, before a cent moves: a real session authenticates (200); no session 401s; **a forged `x-stack-user-id` header authenticates nobody (401)**; an unknown bearer token 401s; a valid session for someone else cannot read another human's wallet (403); a forged-header write 401s and leaves bets/holds/transactions/balance untouched; the web cookie path authenticates and stops authenticating after sign-out; and statically, `lib/auth.ts` contains no `x-stack-user-id`. **Do not add that header back to this script or to `lib/auth.ts`** — it is the forged-identity vulnerability n2 closed.
- **The guard was verified by negative control, not assumed.** `lib/auth.ts` was temporarily patched to honour `x-stack-user-id` again; the script went to **3 failures / exit 1** naming the backdoor, and `identity:check` Assert 6 would fail too. `lib/auth.ts` was then restored byte-for-byte (sha1 `6a22fc63…`) and `jj status` shows it unmodified.
- **Verified:** `npm run verify:payments` 242/242 exit 0 (run twice, both green) · negative control 3 failures exit 1, restored · `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, zero errors · `npm run identity:check` Part A and Part B both PASS · `npm run test:auth` 34/34 (the stub mechanism itself, on a throwaway database) · **no residue in Neon**: a direct count of `vp_%`-prefixed rows across users/groups/group_members/wallets/transactions/escrow_holds/bets/notification_preferences/push_subscriptions and `vp_%` event titles returns 0 in every table after the runs.
- **Interfaces:** `callRoute(handler, url, init)` in this script takes `{ method?, body?, userId?, accessToken?, headers? }` — `userId` means "act as this human with their real session", `accessToken` sends a raw bearer token. New helpers `signIn(userId, username)` / `accessTokenFor(userId)`. No exported/product interface changed.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - A fresh worktree cannot run this: there is no `node_modules` and no `.env.local` in a Rudder workspace, so `npm install` (or borrowing the base checkout's `node_modules`) and copying `.env.local` are prerequisites for every gate in this repo, not just this one [out of lane]
  - This still mutates the shared Neon dev database and relies on prefix-scoped cleanup. `test-auth-consolidation.ts`'s throwaway-database pattern would remove that risk entirely, but `lib/schema.sql` alone may not create the wallet/escrow/transaction tables (they arrive via `scripts/migrate-comeback.ts`), so it needs its own change [out of lane]
  - 11 web client files still send the now-inert `x-stack-user-id` header (n2's own follow-up, unchanged here). Harmless, but they are the reason the string still greps in this repo [out of lane]
  - n10 may now claim payments verified — but only for the paths Step 1–14 cover. Stripe is exercised through signed webhook payloads and a local test key, never a live Stripe account, so deposit *initiation* (`POST /api/wallet` with `action: 'deposit'`, which calls the real Stripe API) is still unproven [out of lane]
- **By:** worker · 2026-08-16
- **Recovered from:** `spawn-a-task-to-fix-scripts-verify-payme-ad696349`

## main: escrow chips now read the same live status on iPhone as on web; the 25 "merge-ready" worktrees hold nothing
- **Merge audit first — nothing was mergeable.** All 30 jj workspaces were compared file-by-file against `main`. Every one is a strict subset or an older ancestor: for each changed path, the workspace copy is either byte-identical to `main` or *older* than it. Several still carry code `main` has already purged — `sendPushToAllSubscribers` (violates the central-notification invariant), the forged `x-stack-user-id` header in `components/Ledger.tsx` (3×), `app/create/page.tsx`, `app/profile/page.tsx` — and `lib/db.ts` without `comment_count`. **Merging any of them would regress landed security invariants.** `main`'s `package.json` already carries every npm script and every `scripts/*.ts` file the fleet produced. The remaining diffs are only append-only `DECISIONS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` / `package.json` churn.
- **Did:** Fixed the real remaining escrow bug, which was on the iPhone half. `mobile/src/screens/EventDetailScreen.tsx` gated its chip on `bet.escrow_hold_id`, a column settlement never clears — so after an event resolved or was cancelled, mobile kept showing **"Escrowed"** on every bet forever, while web correctly showed Settled/Refunded. The screen now reads `event.escrow_by_bet` (the same per-bet map `GET /api/events?id=` already fed the web `Ledger`), with `HOLD_PILLS` mirroring the web's `HOLD_CHIPS` and an `accessibilityLabel` that names whose stake it is.
- **Verified:** `npm run verify:escrow-chips` → **47/47** (was 39/39; 8 new checks) against the live Neon dev DB, all test rows cleaned up. The new step is a live proof, not a grep: it settles a real event and asserts `escrow_hold_id` is **still set** on every settled bet while `escrow_by_bet` reads `released` — the two provably disagree, which is exactly why the old gate was wrong. ## main: 44 stale Rudder runs and all 30 jj workspaces deleted after a content-level audit proved nothing was lost
- **The audit that authorized the deletion.** For every workspace, every changed path, the exact blob was hashed and searched across `main`'s entire history for that path (not just the tip). 92 file-versions were checked. Result: **no workspace held any content that is not already in `main`'s history**, apart from four categories, each individually cleared:
  - `DECISIONS.md` (15 workspaces) — every `## ` heading in every workspace copy is already present in `main`'s 80. The diffs are tail-truncation and ordering only, **zero entries lost**.
  - `package.json` (4 workspaces) — the only scripts `main` lacks are `db:test`, `db:add-push`, `test:push`, `verify-pwa`, whose targets (`scripts/test-activity.ts`, `add-push-subscriptions.ts`, `test-push-notification.ts`, `verify-pwa-setup.ts`) **do not exist anywhere in `main`**. Their removal was deliberate and is already documented in CLAUDE.md §2. No dependency is missing from `main` either.
  - `README.md` (1 workspace) — the 351-line pre-cleanup draft; `main`'s 178-line rewrite covers the same ground (Quick start web/mobile, DB & migrations, env, testing, deployment, troubleshooting).
  - Code files (`components/Ledger.tsx`, `app/events/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/api/events/route.ts`, `lib/db.ts`, `scripts/migrate-comeback.ts`, `scripts/verify-payments.ts`) — all strictly **older** than `main`: they still import `CommentForm` instead of the threaded `CommentThread`, call `sendPushToAllSubscribers` (violates the central-notification invariant), send the forged `x-stack-user-id` header, lack `comment_count`, and lack `migrate-comeback`'s `blocked` counter. Workspace `escrow-chips-only-appear-on-the-viewer-o-8911d91d` even carries **unresolved jj conflict markers inside `package.json`** (invalid JSON). Merging any of them would have regressed landed invariants.
- **Method note — a tooling trap worth remembering.** `jj file show -r REV 'app/events/[id]/page.tsx'` silently returns **empty** for paths containing `[...]`, because the argument is parsed as a fileset and the brackets are glob syntax. A first audit pass compared empty-vs-empty and wrongly reported those Next.js dynamic-route files as identical. **Always use the literal form `jj file show -r REV 'file:"<path>"'`** for any path with brackets; the re-run with that form changed the verdict on 3 files.
- **Did:** Deleted all 44 non-active Rudder runs via `rudder delete` (each removes the run record and its workspace), forgot the one workspace that survived (`rudder-17868937495009-44a31c`), and removed the 16 leftover directory shells under `.rudder-worktrees/` (each held only a generated `RUDDER.md` stub by then). Also repaired the git/jj ref state: the previous session's commit had landed on a **detached HEAD**, so the local `main` ref still pointed at `25253340` while `origin/main` was `35c88ac9` — `main` now matches the remote. Deleted the fully-merged local-only bookmarks `mini-warrior` and `vivacious-minotaurasaurus` (0 commits not in `main`).
- **Result:** `jj workspace list` → `default` only. `rudder runs` → 1 (this session). `.rudder-worktrees/` empty. `jj status` and `git status` clean.
- **Recoverability:** every deleted workspace's change id + commit id was snapshotted before deletion, and jj's op log entry `8377d1f8a20b` predates the whole cleanup, so `jj undo`/`jj op restore` can rewind it.
- **Verified after cleanup, from the clean tree:** `verify:payments` 270/270 · `verify:escrow-chips` 47/47 · `db:verify` 65/65 · `verify:constraints` 20/20 · `verify:imessage` 139/139 · `verify:groups-auth` 77/77 · `verify:users-auth` 49/49 · `test:sync-user` 39/39 · `verify:notifications` 14/14 · `verify:comments` 10/10 · `brand:verify` 26 · `identity:check` Parts A+B · `npm run lint` (warnings only, 0 errors) · `npx tsc --noEmit` clean in root and `mobile/` · `npm run build` succeeds.
- **Follow-ups:**
  - `redesign/midnight-glass` still exists locally and on `origin`. It is fully merged into `main` (0 commits not in `main`), so it can be deleted — left alone because removing a remote branch is the user's call [out of lane]
  - `rudder done` appends its own summary entry to this file on every invocation; three duplicates plus two `probe` entries were appended during this session and reverted by hand. Prefer a single `rudder done` at the very end of a run [out of lane]
